# Theme — Optical / CPO (Co-Packaged Optics & AI Interconnect)

## Recent signals (Aug 13) — 🔴🔴 **BOTH SUPPLIERS NOW REFUTE THE CPO PUSH-OUT ON THE RECORD, ONE OF THEM NAMING THE REPORT — and a second NPO customer gets a ~4-month deadline**

**The push-out thesis is now held by third-party research against the direct statements of both merchant suppliers.** In 48 hours:

- **[[LITE]] (BTG Pactual post-earnings call with a Lumentum executive, Capstone attended, 2026-08-13):** management **explicitly refutes "recent market speculation (e.g. from the SemiAnalysis report)"** of a CPO push-out and reports **INCREASED demand from [[NVDA]] for CPO in the scale-up timeframe starting 2H CY2027**. *"**Nvidia is all in on CPO**,"* which is expected to be **required for rack-to-rack connections in the "Rubin Ultra" timeframe (Phase 1 of CPO scale-up)**, becoming *"a **very robust ramp in CY2028**."*
- **[[COHR]] (FQ4 FY26 call, 2026-08-12; and Barclays' 2026-08-13 review):** CEO Jim Anderson — *"we've seen **absolutely no push-out of CPO demand. In fact, it's been the opposite** — demand INCREASE and demand getting PULLED IN."* Barclays confirms from the outside: **CPO adds revenue in the December quarter for scale-out, "no indication of a push-out"; scale-up CPO 2H CY27** (Barclays · Tom O'Malley, 2026-08-13).

**🔴 NPO — THE SECOND CUSTOMER GETS A DEADLINE, AND THE ARCHITECTURE CLAIM SHARPENS.** Lumentum expects to **announce another "meaningful" NPO customer within ~4 months**, beyond NVIDIA. Mechanics: **NVIDIA uses 400mW lasers for external light sources, absorbing roughly HALF of Lumentum's future Greensboro capacity**; the new engagements may run NPO **"inside the box" with mid-power lasers (120–150mW)** or as ELS. **NPO content ships Q4 CY2027, with a "very robust ramp in CY2028."** ⚠️ **The differentiator: these new NPO opportunities target "scale-up WITHIN the rack," implying HIGHER OPTICAL DENSITY than NVIDIA's initial hybrid approach (rack-to-rack optical, in-rack copper).** CEO Hurlston, separately at the JPM HW/Semis forum the same morning: **~6 meaningful NPO engagements**, and *"**the intensity of those engagements (# of optical lanes) is actually HIGHER than NVDA** — # of NPO optical lanes from north to south is considerably higher — more opportunity, more customers."* His framing of the substitution: *"**NPO gets you a lot of the performance cheaper (Tesla 3 versus Model X); but eventually everyone will have to go to CPO.** Also building NPO helps develop confidence and competence, so will lead to NPO players moving confidently to CPO over time"* (JPM · Joshua Meyers, raw notes, 2026-08-13). **[[COHR]] independently: NPO/CPO engagements up significantly over 3–6 months, content "very comparable" between the two, "final plans of customers still in flux"** (MS · Meta Marshall, 2026-08-13) — and **[[AAOI]]'s CFO/CSO Stefan Murray takes the other side on timing: *"there's a disconnect between the speed at which investors think things happen and what actually needs to happen to bring CPO to market at scale… End-2027 they expect to start and see some orders, but it's a 2028 story,"* while still seeing **CPO as the major player over NPO** and reporting **no change in NPO demand** (JPM forum, 2026-08-13).**

**🔴 THE CAPACITY LAYER — THE CONSTRAINT REMAINS INDIUM PHOSPHIDE, AND THE TOOL VENDOR JUST CONFIRMED IT FROM THE OTHER END.** [[COHR]]: InP output **on track to double by F1Q27-end and more than double again by CY27-end**, with **InP laser production +80% y/y in F4Q26 underpinning data-center revenue growth "to exceed +80% y/y" in F1Q27** (JPM · Cardoso, 2026-08-13) — a **one-quarter lead from laser output to datacom revenue**, the cleanest transmission rule this theme has. BofA adds **>75% of COHR capacity on 6-inch by CY27, "4x supply at half the cost"** (2026-08-13). ✅ **And from the equipment side: [[VECO]] disclosed a $200M advanced-packaging order plus an additional LUMINA+ MOCVD order, "incremental to the $250M+ InP order from last quarter," while investing to MORE THAN DOUBLE capacity in AP and SiPho — with capacity arriving closer to 2H CY27 (Barclays · O'Malley, 2026-08-13). The tool orders and the supplier ramps are consistent; the timing gap (tools land 2H CY27) is the thing to watch.**

**⚠️ THE NEW BEAR THREAD IS A SUBSTITUTION QUESTION, NOT A DEMAND QUESTION — and it comes from a bull.** Barclays on [[COHR]]: *"**800G volume in CY27 continues to be significant but UNCLEAR AT THIS POINT IF IT GROWS amidst the 1.6T ramp**"* (2026-08-13). ➜ **If 1.6T cannibalises rather than layers on top of 800G, unit growth decouples from revenue growth across the transceiver complex. Note this cuts the OTHER way on margin: LITE puts 1.6T transceiver GM at 30–35% vs 25% for 800G, so mix substitution is margin-accretive even where it is unit-neutral (BTG call, 2026-08-13).**

**⚠️ POLICY — THE FCC/CHINA BAN IS NOT A CLEAN POSITIVE FOR THE US SUPPLIERS, AND LUMENTUM SAID SO.** ~**65% of the world's transceivers come from Chinese-headquartered companies**; a ban would be *"very disruptive for the industry"* short-term, and the definition is messy (**manufacturing location vs company HQ**). Lumentum *"would like to have this business"* but it **"needs to be margin-sensible, as current transceiver margins might not justify taking on all volume"** — and the company is lobbying for *"a more gradual and thoughtful shift,"* partly to protect its **significant Chinese laser-chip customers** (BTG call, 2026-08-13). BofA reaches the same conclusion from the outside on [[COHR]]: *"considering a potential Chinese transceiver ban, COHR would be in the **best position to benefit** as it is **third in global share** and has a large US-based footprint"* (2026-08-13). **[[AAOI]]'s read on Washington's intent is the most specific: the government "wants to make sure that Chinese growth in the transceiver market doesn't become a threat," but is "also very focused on ensuring that US AI growth isn't crippled… They are not saying it needs to be 100% in the US; but they want a meaningful part of it to be here." The mechanism floated: "originally approved designs continue to be allowed to import; but as newer generations come out, you could seek to change the supply chain" (JPM forum, 2026-08-13).**

**Read-through to the ban's beneficiaries, with a caveat:** [[AAOI]] — *"customers are willing to pay a premium for US capacity"*; **400G a slight margin drag, 800G accretive, 1.6T better still; margins to mid-30% by mid-2027, and "1.6T and even more margin-accretive CPO could take them to 40%+ GMs over time"**; **all production now on 4-inch wafer, with 6-inch "not necessary/desirable for at least a year or two… ultimately an economic/cost decision"** — ⚠️ **a deliberate divergence from COHR's 6-inch strategy, and worth tracking as a cost-position gap if the InP shortage persists** (JPM forum, 2026-08-13).

**A ONE-LINE SUPPLY-CHAIN DEMAND READ-THROUGH FROM AN OFF-COVERAGE NAME — datacenter signal-integrity/connectivity content is compounding at a rate consistent with everything else on this page: **Semtech (SMTC)**'s datacenter revenue runs from roughly **$13MM in C1Q23 to $97MM in C1Q26** (UBS · Timothy Arcuri, with Natalia Winkler / Alex Kivali, "Semtech Corp — Increasing Estimates Following Cellular Module Sale", Buy, 12m PT $225, px $134.92, 2026-08-13, Figure 1) [Source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/ued50151.html).** ⚠️ **OFF-COVERAGE, NO WIKI PAGE — logged as a demand datapoint only. The note itself is about divesting the low-margin cellular module business and is OFF-THEME; nothing else from it is carried here. The intermediate quarters in the exhibit came through the text layer scrambled and are deliberately NOT quoted — endpoints only.**

## Recent signals (Aug 12) — 🔴 **NPO GETS A DATE AND A DEFINITION ("ADDITIVE TAM, LATE-2027 SCALE-UP RAMP"), THE SUPPLIER'S SHIPMENT CLOCK IS DECOUPLED FROM NVIDIA'S DEPLOYMENT CLOCK, AND [[COHR]] PRINTS WITH GROSS MARGIN GUIDED *UP***

**The morning-after sweep on [[LITE]]'s print (JPM, Jefferies, MS, Barclays, R&Co Redburn — all 2026-08-12) plus [[COHR]]'s own print that evening moved four of this theme's open questions.**

- **🔴 NPO IS NOW DEFINED AS ADDITIVE, NOT SUBSTITUTIONAL, AND IT HAS A RAMP DATE.** Management: **NPO is additive to the TAM, ramping in late 2027 for scale-up applications**, while **CPO production plans "remain on track, with an increased demand signal"** (JPM / Joseph Cardoso, 2026-08-12). Barclays: **scale-up CPO at the lead customer ("we think [[NVDA]]") on track for 2H CY27 shipments / CY28 deployments**, with other customers *and* NVDA looking at NPO **"which will be incremental to LITE's scale-up optical opportunities"** (Tom O'Malley, 2026-08-12).
- **🔴 THE MID-POWER-LASER QUESTION — this theme's sharpest open dispute — GETS A COMPANY ANSWER (not evidence, but on the record).** MS: management sees **"a wide variety of engagements on NPO, even from their lead CPO customer,"** spanning **mid-power to high-power lasers**, and cautions that **"one shouldn't naturally assume that competitive differentiation / margin opportunity is less with the mid-power lasers"** (Meta Marshall, 2026-08-12). ⚠️ **This directly answers the Jefferies challenge logged on 08-07 — *"these are shorter scale-up connections — you might not need a high-powered laser"* — which was the single largest threat to the laser-content thesis in this theme. The dispute is NOT settled (management assertion vs analyst engineering judgement), but the supplier has now taken a public position and can be held to it.**
- **🔴 THE SUPPLIER/CUSTOMER TIMING DECOUPLING — new, and it changes how CPO-slip headlines should be traded:** *"while NVDA's plans for scale up deployments may push to **1H 2028**, **LITE's timeline has not changed, with ultra-high power laser shipments still expected to begin in 2H27**"* (Jefferies / Blayne Curtis, 2026-08-12). ➜ **Because the capacity is under take-or-pay, the component maker ships to its own schedule even if the deployment slips. Every prior CPO-pushout headline on this page was implicitly modelled as a 1:1 revenue deferral for the suppliers. That assumption is wrong.**
- **A CONTENT UNIT, from the only house that published one: R&Co Redburn (Mike Harrison) — "we estimate 25m lasers per GW" for scale-up CPO** (2026-08-12). ⚠️ **File as a single-source estimate. It is the first per-GW laser-content figure in this theme and is worth cross-checking against the Aurelion/Global Semi Research channel numbers already here (NVDA guiding LITE+COHR to ~40MM CW units in January rising to ~100MM by April/May, back-solving to 200–300k CPO switches in 2027 and 600–800k in 2028) — the two framings are not obviously reconcilable and should not be blended.**
- **🔴 THE MARGIN QUESTION SPLITS IN TWO, and the split is the useful part:**
  - **Structural/long-run: [[LITE]] RAISED its out-year operating-margin target to 40–44% (from 38–42%)** (JPM, 2026-08-12) — the first move in that frame since the March analyst day.
  - **Near-term: Jefferies dates the offsetting drag — "the steep ramp of that lower-margin [Cloud Light / transceiver] revenue likely softens the potential for margin expansion over the NTM" before scale-up UHP unfolds in 2H27.**
  - **And [[COHR]] guided the OTHER way: FQ1 FY27 gross margin 41.5% vs Street 40.0%, i.e. UP, on a print that also delivered GM +215bps y/y and op margin +381bps y/y** (COHR release, 2026-08-12). ⚠️ **Two optical primes, same week, both guiding margin higher into a transceiver ramp. That is material evidence against the "transceiver mix inevitably dilutes" bear this theme has carried. The ~10pt GM gap between LITE (50.4%) and COHR (40.2%) is unchanged — but the DIRECTION of travel is now the same for both.**
- **✅ SUPPLY REMAINS THE BINDING CONSTRAINT, restated with numbers (Jefferies, 2026-08-12): EML demand still running 30%+ ahead of supply; pump lasers SOLD OUT against a planned 4x increase underwritten by largely take-or-pay; 200G EMLs above 25% of EML revenue on a path to majority volume by mid-2027; 1.6T Tx adoption intensifying from fiscal Q1.** MS adds the mix math: **200G at ~25% of units and "probably more like 40% of revenue," from 5%/10% two quarters ago.**
- **🔴 OCS — a competitive datapoint that surprised the analyst who reported it: "the view that they will win MAJORITY SHARE AT GOOGLE in C27 surprised us"** (Jefferies, 2026-08-12), alongside **LITE's first triple-digit OCS revenue quarter in F1Q** and **no upside to the $400M 2H26 figure**.
- **⚠️ THE CHINA/InP SUPPLY BEAR GETS A MANAGEMENT REBUTTAL (assertion, unverified): LITE has seen "no impact from newly announced Chinese InP capacity," citing differentiation in EMLs, high-/mid-power lasers and even CW lasers "where it is pricing UP on superior yields," and notes several Chinese suppliers "are not yet delivering into the market"** (JPM, 2026-08-12). Same evidentiary caveat as the 07-29 IR meeting — this is the company grading its own competition.
- **⚠️ NON-AI SOFTNESS, corroborated across two issuers in one week: [[COHR]]'s Industrial segment fell short of expectations, and [[LITE]] reported Industrial Tech flat q/q with continued softness in Industrial Laser.** ➜ **Treat as an industrial end-market signal, not company noise.**
- **Read-through to the buyers of this content: [[MRVL]] previews into a 2Q print where GS expects a CY26/27 guide raise "driven by higher hyperscaler CapEx, strong optical networking demand, sustained momentum at existing ASIC customers, and the ramp of a new custom silicon customer"** (GS / James Schneider, 2026-08-12).

## Recent signals (Aug 11, /run-inbox — PRIMARY PDF) — GOLDMAN TAKES THE **CHINESE INCUMBENTS'** SIDE OF THE IMPORT-BAN DEBATE, AND PUBLISHES THE FULL MODULE TAM: **$34bn (2025) → $51bn (2026E) → $73bn (2027E) → $69bn (2028E)** — note the 2028 DECLINE
- **The note is scoped exactly to the ban thread this page opened on 08-04: "the Trump administration is drafting a ban on US imports of Chinese data center components, with the FCC working on measures to bar imports of Chinese made optical modules" — and Goldman is explicit that it takes NO VIEW on the outcome while staying positive on the CHINESE leaders (Buy on FOCI, RoboTechnik, Landmark, Eoptolink, VPEC)** (Goldman Sachs · Allen Chang / Verena Jeng / Ting Song, "Optical Networking: Top 3 investor debates on impact of potential US import restrictions on Chinese optical transceiver modules", 2026-08-11). ⚠️ **Recorded as the counter-position to this page's Western-optics rally read: the same event that the desks traded as a [[COHR]]/[[LITE]]/[[AAOI]] positive is being argued, by the analyst closest to the Chinese supply chain, to leave the incumbents' share INTACT. No US name is discussed in the note — no company page was patched from it, deliberately.**
- 🔴 **THE TAM TABLE, which is the most granular module forecast on this page and the first with a 3.2T line:**

| US$m | 2025 | 2026E | 2027E | 2028E |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| **Total module TAM** | **34,211** | **50,883** | **72,593** | **69,135** |
| Below 400G | 16,827 | 16,886 | 16,166 | 13,196 |
| 400G | 4,209 | 1,745 | 969 | 287 |
| 800G | 9,318 | 13,530 | 14,715 | 10,761 |
| 1.6T | 3,857 | 18,722 | 27,403 | 23,264 |
| 3.2T | — | — | 13,340 | 21,625 |

⚠️ **THE 2028 DECLINE IS THE ITEM TO ARGUE WITH, because nothing else on this page has it: total TAM falls 5% in 2028E even as UNIT shipments rise (403mn → 452mn → 528mn → 556mn). The mechanism is ASP — Goldman assumes like-for-like price declines every year (blended ASP $85 → $113 → $137 → $124; 800G $440 → $396 → $327 → $244; 1.6T $1,342 → $904 → $734 → $435; 3.2T $768 in 2028) that eventually outrun mix. Whether 2027 is the revenue peak for this industry is now a live, dated question rather than a vibe.**
- **Mix and materials, which is the CPO-adjacent part: 1.6T goes 11% → 37% → 38% → 34% of TAM value and 3.2T enters at 18% (2027E) rising to 31% (2028E). SiPh overtakes EML on VALUE in 2027 (56% vs 44%, from 28% in 2025) while SiPh UNIT penetration is only 6% → 10% → 15% → 18% — i.e. SiPh is concentrated in the high-ASP tiers, which is the quantitative version of the CPO/SiPh migration thesis this page carries.** **AI-rack context in the same note (chart read): NVL72-class racks 19k (2025) → 50k → 92k → 148k units, a 118% CAGR 2025-28E, with AMD NVL72-class at 5k → 13k → 15k; general servers +18% CAGR; AI servers (8-GPU) +26% CAGR.**
- **Goldman's three debates, answered in the incumbents' favour: (1) WHY LEADERS KEEP WINNING — "such a fast evolving technology environment only INCREASES customers' reliance on existing leaders rather than reducing it," across speeds (1.6T/2.4T/3.2T), form factors (pluggable/LPO/NPO/CPO), materials (SiPh/EML/lithium niobate) and fiber types; the SKU count itself is the moat. (2) CAPACITY & COST — "SEVEN OUT OF THE GLOBAL TOP 10 optical module suppliers by revenue are based in China and EXPANDED market share in 2025 vs 2024," on self-designed production lines and proprietary equipment; and the 1.6T-and-above step-up "add[s] difficulties in design but ALSO IN MANUFACTURING, given that the SIZE of optical modules is NOT INCREASING while at the same time requiring MORE FIBERS / LASERS PER BOX… adding difficulties to coupling and thermal dissipation." (3) SITE DIVERSIFICATION — Thailand and SEA capacity is already producing high-end 1.6T, with "Eoptolink… expanding PHASE 2 capacity in Thailand in 2026E with PHASE 1 ALREADY OPERATING AT FULL UTILIZATION RATE."** ➤ **That last point is the sharpest practical objection to the ban thesis on this page: the relocation is not prospective, it is running at full utilisation — so an import ban keyed to Chinese ORIGIN rather than Chinese OWNERSHIP has a well-developed workaround already in production. Cross-filed [china-export](china-export.md).** [Source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Optical_Networking__Top_3_investor_debates_on_impact_of_potential_US_import_restrictions_on_Chinese_optical_transceiver_.html)

## Recent signals (Aug 11) — 🔴 [[LITE]] PRINTS A BEAT-AND-RAISE AND THE STOCK GOES NOWHERE (THE BAR IS NOW THE WHISPER, NOT CONSENSUS); MANAGEMENT PUTS **IN-RACK** OPTICS ON THE BOARD FOR THE FIRST TIME; AND **SCALE-ACROSS / COHERENT** GETS ITS OWN ARCHITECTURE WRITE-UP

**🔴 (1) THE PRINT: [[LITE]] BEAT EVERY LINE AND GUIDED SEPTEMBER ~8% ABOVE THE STREET, AND TRADED FLAT-TO-DOWN.** Q4FY26 **revenue $1.006–1.010B** vs Street $988–990M · **GM 50.4%** vs 48.5–48.8% · **OM 36.6%** vs 35.2–35.5% · **EPS $3.23** vs $2.97. **F1Q27 guide $1.225–1.275B (mid $1.250B) vs Street $1.151–1.16B; OM 39.5–40.5%; EPS $4.05–4.35 (mid $4.20) vs $3.58–3.61** — reaching the target model *"more than a quarter ahead of schedule."* Stock **-0.4% to -0.7%** AH.
- **🔴 WHY IT MATTERS FOR THE WHOLE COMPLEX, and this is the transferable lesson into [[COHR]]'s 08-12 print: "the tepid response feels like an EXPECTATIONS PROBLEM: last week, a few folks said my Bars seemed aggressive, but BY THE END OF THE DAY TODAY FOLKS WERE SAYING THE COMPANY HAD TO DO $1.3b IN REVENUE GUIDE TO WORK… so this feels somewhat REMINISCENT OF THE TXN PRINT"** (JPM / Joshua Meyers, "LITE… First Blush", 2026-08-11). The EPS beat was **13c ahead of JPM's Buyside Bars** and **GM beat consensus by 610bps.** ➜ **In this group, clearing published research is no longer sufficient — the marginal buyer is trading off an intraday whisper. Both UBS and Vital Knowledge reached the same verdict independently ("presumably on elevated expectations"; "the numbers and guide are both great, but expectations were fairly elevated").**
- **Context on the week: LITE and COHR had fallen -9% / -14% on Monday 08-10 on the FCC/China-ban speculation, which Jefferies could not source — *"didn't come across any good reasons tbh (perhaps all the long optical short memory chorus on X over the weekend was a reverse indicator?)"* — while noting sell-side notes arguing the FCC ban is unlikely to go through, and that Chinese optical names Innolight/Eoptolink were UP +3%/+4% overnight** (Jefferies / Rankie Wong, 2026-08-11). **Also flagged: Innolight-H was the #1 bought HK-listed stock by Korean retail since its 30-Jul listing.**

**🔴 (2) NET-NEW FROM MANAGEMENT: OPTICS ARE STARTING TO PENETRATE **IN-RACK** CONNECTIVITY, AND THERE IS A FIRST **ELS MODULE** ORDER.** Hurlston, in the Q4FY26 release: *"key growth drivers such as **OCS solutions and our cloud module business, where we are advancing 1.6T adoption**, are beginning to layer in. **Increasing demand for ultra-high-power CPO lasers, an INITIAL ORDER FOR ELS MODULES, as well as our breadth of NPO ENGAGEMENTS are the first signs that OPTICS ARE STARTING TO PENETRATE IN-RACK CONNECTIVITY, SIGNIFICANTLY UPPING OUR OPTICAL TAM.**"* ➜ **This page has dated scale-up CPO variously to late-2027 (LITE IR), ~2030 (LITE's own SiPh R&D Director) and 2028/29 (buy-side). Management has now given the first *qualitative* evidence of in-rack penetration plus a first ELS order — but NO dated ramp and NO quantified TAM revision. The dating spread survives the print intact; the direction is confirmed.**
- **The pre-print buy-side view, for the record (Papa Sylla, expert/client call relayed 2026-08-11, hours before the print):** wanted **scale-out CPO with [[NVDA]]'s Spectrum switches visible in the September guide**; estimates **LITE's CPO run-rate at ~$100M in cal-3Q and $250M+ in cal-4Q** (his estimate, hard to map 1:1 given internal-vs-supplied mix); dates **scale-up CPO to late 2028/2029**; reiterates **LITE sold out through 2028** supporting **~15% price increases**; puts LITE share at **~35–40%**; and argues a China ban is *"tactical negotiation"* because **China supplies ~70% of the rare-earth feedstock and would retaliate on lasers.** ⚠️ **But: even absent a ban, de-risking could take hyperscaler allocation to LITE from 40% → 30% over two years, which he frames as a positive for [[COHR]] — bounded by the fact that COHR only wins if China does not retaliate, and that LITE is itself a large laser customer.** Growth frame he attributes to the street: FY26→27 **+100%**, FY27→28 **+80%**.

**🔴 (3) SCALE-ACROSS GETS A DEDICATED ARCHITECTURE WRITE-UP, AND IT IS A COHERENT-OPTICS STORY (DIGITIMES research, "AI infrastructure moves toward scale-across; demand for coherent optics supply chain heats up", 2026-08-11).**
- **The driver is physical, not architectural preference:** *"**Because a single data center faces limits in power supply, floor space, and cooling, hyperscalers are distributing GPU clusters across campus data centers 2-20 kilometers apart**, then linking them with high-speed optical networks so that dispersed computing resources can work together for AI model training and inference."*
- **The technology fork:** *"**As AI interconnect bandwidth rises to 800G and 1.6T, traditional intensity modulation/direct detection (IMDD) technology is increasingly insufficient for high-capacity, long-distance data transmission, making COHERENT OPTICS THE MAINSTREAM TECHNOLOGY FOR SCALE-ACROSS.**"*
- **🔴 The cost problem and the two named answers, which is the part worth carrying:** coherent optics were built for long-haul backbone and are expensive, so vendors are rolling out **(a) "COHERENT LITE" — lower module cost via simplified DSPs and relaxed optical-component specs, targeting 1.6T campus DCI; and (b) "MULTI-RAIL" — higher line-system density, sharing cabinets, power and cooling across multiple rails, cutting repeater-hut deployment cost.** *"Scale-across optical supply chain led by [[MRVL]], [[CIEN]], and others."* ⚠️ **COHR/LITE are not named in the available excerpt — treat this as TAM/architecture evidence and a supplier-league datapoint for MRVL/CIEN, not as a share claim for the US laser names.**
- ➜ **Why this matters to this page's central debate: scale-across is a THIRD demand vector alongside scale-out and scale-up, it is a coherent/DSP story rather than an InP-laser story, and it arrives on a 2–20km distance regime where copper was never a competitor. It also explains the [[APH]]/CommScope datapoint the same day — "demand is strongest in advanced optical interconnect for AI SCALE-OUT AND SCALE-ACROSS" (MS / Meta Marshall, 2026-08-11).**

**(4) THE FIBER LAYER: THREE COMPANIES, ONE SHORTAGE, THREE DIFFERENT OUTCOMES (MS / Meta Marshall · Antonio Jaramillo, 2026-08-11).** **Fujikura is taking "like-for-like price increases of ~25% in some instances" for shorter lead times, with its Sakura plant near full utilisation and external sourcing of fiber and preforms bridging supply (+7.6% on the guide raise); [[APH]]/CommScope "retains adequate supply… without constraining shipments" and had FY26 revenue raised to $4.6B from $4.1B with EPS accretion doubled to $0.30 from $0.15 on IT-datacom nearly doubling y/y; [[GLW]] cannot monetise the scarcity at all because its capacity is committed under hyperscaler LTAs — "GLW's limited pricing commentary looks more reflective of capacity committed under LTAs than weaker demand."** Capex intensity for the same buildout: **GLW's $2.0B FY26 plan implies 8.3% of sales in 1H rising to ~12.6% in 2H, vs APH "near or above the high end of its 3-4% of sales range."** ➜ **The generalisable point: in this shortage, price accrues to whoever is NOT under a long-term agreement. That is the mirror image of the LTA argument running on [hbm-memory.md](hbm-memory.md), and worth holding side by side.**
- **New structural demand leg, bounded by the analyst: the [[NVDA]]-backed long-haul build — 16 US routes built or overbuilt, NVDA leasing fiber on 8 existing routes, new builds designed for ≥1,728 fibers per route, "~15mm fiber miles vs LUMN total of ~58mm miles today… only ~25% of the size, but we still see as a positive."**

## Recent signals (Aug 10, PM) — 🔴 NVIDIA'S CPO LEG GETS DATED TO **NVL576**, NOT NVL72; JAPAN'S OPTICAL SEASON CLOSES WITH TWO GUIDE HIKES IN UNDER TWO MONTHS; AND THE TWO US NAMES PRINT THIS WEEK INTO A ~50% RALLY

**🔴 (1) THE CPO TIMELINE FINALLY GETS ATTACHED TO A SPECIFIC RACK SKU (Morgan Stanley Asia · Charlie Chan · Daniel Yen · Daisy Dai, "AI Supply Chain: GPU Architecture Optimization Given Memory Shortage and Supernode Challenge", 2026-08-10).** *"Given ongoing **PCB and thermal challenges**, **first-generation Rubin Ultra server racks will likely continue to use the OBERON solution for NVL72** while **adopting CPO/NPO technologies to enable NVL576-scale deployments**."* MS remains *"optimistic about Nvidia's adoption of CPO as a **key differentiator in scale-up performance versus China's supernode architectures**"* and confirms **inter-rack CPO connectivity as Nvidia's preferred long-term direction**, with **Kyber blade delayed with no concrete timeline**.
- ⚠️ **Why this is the most decision-useful CPO datapoint of the week:** this page has carried "CPO is in mass production" (NVDA, Aug 3) alongside "the near-term architecture is NPO" (MS, Aug 3) as an unresolved pair. **The reconciliation is now explicit and it is a PACKAGING-TIER split, not a timing fudge — CPO/NPO rides NVL576, pluggables + Oberon carry NVL72 through the first Rubin Ultra generation.** For the component names that means **conventional pluggable capacity and yields are what get paid this cycle**, with CPO content dated to the NVL576 window. Cross-ref [../NVDA.md](../NVDA.md), [../LITE.md](../LITE.md), [../COHR.md](../COHR.md).

**🔴 (2) JAPAN'S OPTICAL EARNINGS SEASON ENDED WITH THE CLEANEST DEMAND SIGNAL OF THE MONTH — AND IT IS THE GUIDE-HIKE CADENCE, NOT THE BEAT (Morgan Stanley desk · Amir Amerian, 2026-08-10).**
- **Fujikura: 1Q OP 42% above consensus, and FY OP raised to ¥432bn (cons ¥330bn, MSRe ¥345bn)** — *"it **again** raised its FY OP… after raising its guide above-cons in June"*, i.e. **two above-consensus hikes in under two months**, explicitly on *"**higher probability of winning large-scale projects in FY 2H**."* Stock **+~14% Friday, +7% overnight**.
- **Furukawa Electric: 1Q OP 50% above consensus**, FY OP raised to **¥123bn (cons ¥101bn, MSRe ¥100bn)** on *"wider sales of DC-related products in **Optical Solutions and Digital Infrastructure Components**."* Stock **+~12%** and holding.
- **Sumitomo Electric missed** in its key Information & Communication segment — but *"for **company-specific reason** (certain operations were taken off longer than expected during **capacity expansion**)."* ⚠️ **A capacity-expansion outage, not a demand miss — do not read it as a sector datapoint.**
- **The layer below corroborates:** **Anritsu** (KEYS peer) posted a **large 1Q ORDERS beat from optical-transceiver TESTING demand**; **Misumi revised up F3/27 China Telecom revenue by 48%** (read: China transceiver); **HOYA** "Optical Solutions" revenue **+22% JPY / +11% y/y cc to ¥16.5bn**, with **CUPO polarization glass for optical transceivers the primary growth driver**. ⚠️ Note the **test-equipment orders beat is the leading indicator** — it precedes module shipment revenue by two-plus quarters.

**(3) THE WEEK'S EVENT RISK IS CONCENTRATED IN TWO PRINTS, AND THE BAR HAS MOVED.** *"For the optical trade to keep working, **it's all up to [[LITE]] & [[COHR]] this week**"* (LITE Tue 08-11, COHR Wed 08-12). MS (Meta Marshall) previews **both slightly cautious** with buyside expectations *"balanced"*, sees **more margin upside for COHR**, expects **less chance LITE formally pulls forward its Analyst Day targets**, and warns *"this set of results **will not solve the longer-term debates**: **execution on the CPO ramp and durability of margins**."* Managements expected to emphasise **NPO over CPO** — consistent with (1) above. Buy-side framing: *"**both of these stocks are up around 50% since the late-July lows**"* (Crux Capital / Gaetano, 2026-08-10). **UBS is the outlier with a Neutral on LITE (PT $960, David Vogt): "growth in the Sept and Dec quarter is likely to be 'PEAKISH' as comps become much tougher,"** and — against this page's substrate thesis — cites *"**increasing supply of InP**"* as one of the two things pressuring the multiple. Cross-ref [../AXTI.md](../AXTI.md).

**(4) ⚠️ THE CHINA-RESTRICTION CATALYST IS STILL UNPUBLISHED RULE-MAKING, AND THE DESK CLOSEST TO IT SAYS SO.** Carried forward from the 08-04 reports that *"the Trump administration and FCC are preparing restrictions on new Chinese data-center components, with **optical transceivers specifically mentioned**"* — **no formal rule has been published**, though *"a rule could go into effect this year."* Tape on the day: **COHR +12%, FN +16%, LITE +8% vs [[AXTI]] −5%**; **[[GLW]] +9%**, though MS notes *"there is **not a lot of Chinese fiber in US data centers already**"* — i.e. a weaker GLW catalyst than the move implied. Marshall's substantive point is a supply one: *"there **isn't enough supply from non-Chinese companies to meet global AI capex needs**,"* and **US CSPs have been qualifying more non-Chinese suppliers** in anticipation. **Both LITE and COHR have publicly advocated for tighter controls.** ⚠️ **The desk's own hedge is the right one to carry: "let's see if this is posturing into the Trump-Xi September meeting."** Named beneficiaries on the InP/laser layer if it lands: **Sumitomo Electric (InP substrate, EML, CW lasers) and JX Advanced Metals (InP substrate)** — with **AXTI and Sumitomo Electric the dominant global InP substrate suppliers**, and **JX's InP capacity expansion re-framed from a laser-margin risk into a non-China supply positive.** (Morgan Stanley desk / Amir Amerian, 2026-08-04, re-circulated 2026-08-10.)

## Recent signals (Aug 7) — 🔴 A STREET-HIGH INITIATION TAKES THE *OTHER* SIDE OF THE CPO-DISPLACEMENT BEAR, AAOI PUTS A NUMBER ON UNFILLED DEMAND (20-40%), AND NVIDIA'S NETWORKING SVP SPEAKS ON CPO vs PLUGGABLES

**🔴 (1) THE CPO-DISPLACEMENT BEAR IS NOW CONTESTED BY THE STREET HIGH.** BofA initiated [[TSEM]] at **BUY with a street-high PO of $367 / ILS 1,020 (63% upside)**, and point three of the thesis attacks the standing bear directly: **"CPO, THE HOLY GRAIL of optical connectivity expected in coming years, IS FRAMED AS A MAJOR SHARE LOSS RISK TO TOWER BECAUSE OF [[TSM]] TAKING SHARE, BUT WE BELIEVE TOWER HAS A GREAT OPPORTUNITY IN CPO BECAUSE OF ITS SUPERIOR PIC TECHNOLOGY."** BofA describes Tower as holding **"MARKET-LEADING SHARE in manufacturing the SILICON PHOTONICS (SiPho) chips used for optical connectivity in AI data centers,"** with **SiPho revenue DOUBLING in 2026E**, **"UPSIDE POTENTIAL TO TOWER'S LATEST 2028 GUIDE"** on demand clearly exceeding supply, and **"NEXT-GENERATION NPO AND XPO OPTICAL ARCHITECTURES"** expanding the TAM. Numbers: **62% EBITDA CAGR 2025-28E, 13%/28% above consensus on FY27/FY28E EPS, PO = 20x FY28E EV/EBITDA** (BofA · Oliver Wong, 2026-08-07). ⚠️ **"Superior PIC technology" is ASSERTED, not quantified, in the captured text — log the disagreement, not a resolution. Also note "XPO," a category this wiki has not previously carried alongside NPO/CPO.**

**🔴 (2) THE SUPPLY/DEMAND GAP GETS A NUMBER, FROM THE COMPANY THAT REPORTED FIRST.** From the [[AAOI]] Q2 call: **"DEMAND IS 20%-40% HIGHER THAN WHAT AAOI CAN PROVIDE"** and **"EXPECTED CAPACITY THROUGH Q2 2027 IS ALREADY SPOKEN FOR,"** with customers **"repeatedly asking them to PULL DELIVERIES FORWARD… almost every week."** Capacity **DOUBLED in one quarter** (800G/1.6T from ~100K units/month exiting Q1 to ~200K today), year-end target **650K units/month** on track, and **"most of the future capacity growth will be U.S. BASED"** (Crux Capital / Gaetano, 2026-08-06). ⚠️ **An independent Substack author's account of the call — not a broker note, not a transcript. But the 20-40% figure is the first published sizing of unfilled optical demand on this page, and if right it is a direct read-across into [[COHR]] and [[LITE]], both reporting next week.**
- 🔴 **AND A THREE-LAYER MEMORY PROPAGATION, which is the most re-usable fact of the week: AAOI's Q3 guide was cut by "a $20M-$25M DECLINE IN 100G REV BECAUSE A CUSTOMER CANNOT SOURCE ENOUGH SWITCHES DUE TO MEMORY CONSTRAINTS… WITHOUT THAT HEADWIND, Q3 SEQUENTIAL GROWTH WOULD HAVE EXCEEDED 50%." Optics losing revenue because switches cannot get memory. Cross-file [hbm-memory](hbm-memory.md).**
- **COMPONENT BOTTLENECKS NAMED, and immediately contradicted: AAOI cites "TIA AND DSP COMPONENT CONSTRAINTS," with the read-through "on [[AVGO]], [[MRVL]], [[CRDO]], MXL, SMTC, MTSI."** ⚠️ **[[QCOM]] IR pushed back the same day on exactly this point: "we haven't said who we are partnering with on lasers but WE FEEL GOOD ABOUT THE RAMP, DON'T HAVE ANY BOTTLENECKS." Both logged — the disagreement is itself the datapoint, since one of them is wrong about a component shortage in the same quarter.**
- **CPO, from AAOI: "working closely with AT LEAST 5 CUSTOMERS. SEVERAL OF THEM ALREADY QUALIFIED THEIR LASERS FOR CPO. IT'S A PRODUCTION CAPACITY ISSUE RATHER THAN PERFORMANCE/CUSTOMER INTEREST."** Margin structure if it lands: **laser GM ~55-65%, ESLFP >50%.** ⚠️ **Management's "best laser in the industry/world" claim is not adopted; the author himself declines to weight it.**
- **SUBSTRATE, the [[AXTI]]-adjacent constraint: "SUBSTRATE SUPPLY IS BASICALLY SECURED THROUGH THE END OF 2027" across 2 European / 2 Japanese / 3 Chinese suppliers; AAOI is "TALKING WITH 2-3 SUBSTRATE SUPPLIERS ABOUT DEEPER PARTNERSHIPS, POTENTIALLY EVEN A JOINT VENTURE," because "LASER CAPACITY COULD GROW BY WELL OVER 10x OVER THE NEXT FEW YEARS."** ⚠️ **The author's domestic-InP-JV scenario built from there is EXPLICITLY his own speculation and is not adopted.**

**(3) THE PREVIEWS SET THE BOGEYS, and the same analyst is skeptical of the timing he is underwriting (Jefferies · Blayne Curtis, "Blayne's Bytes", 2026-08-07).** **[[LITE]] reports Tuesday PM, [[COHR]] Wednesday PM.**
- **LITE: "we expect a BEAT AND RAISE with SEPTEMBER GUIDED TO $1.25B+ (VS ST. $1.16B)." "SCALE-UP CPO/NPO REMAINS THE BIGGEST DRIVER AND DEBATE, WITH SHIPMENTS STARTING 2H C27 AND GREENSBORO SUPPORTING >$5B OF INCREMENTAL REVENUE, THOUGH WE'RE SKEPTICAL ON TIMING GIVEN THE KYBER PUSH-OUT."** 🔴 **That is the honest form of the CPO debate: the SIZE is not contested, the DATE is — and the named cause is checkable.**
- **COHR: "we also see a BEAT AND RAISE (revenue guided to LDD growth vs St. +8%), with THE 6-INCH InP TRANSITION THE KEY UNLOCK AS INTERNAL OUTPUT LOOKS SET TO DOUBLE BY 3Q26, A QUARTER AHEAD OF PLAN."** ✅ **This is a company-specific escape from the theme-wide InP tightness logged on 08-05/08-06 — if it holds, COHR is structurally less exposed to the [[AXTI]]/China substrate chokepoint than LITE or the merchant module makers.**
- **Setup and the standing overhang: both names "UNDERPERFORMED SINCE APRIL 1ST — SOX +56% vs COHR +31% and LITE +11%"; both at ~22x C28 EPS of $15 / $37, +75% / +130% YTD. "[[AMZN]] AND [[MSFT]] RESULTS OFFERED PROOF POINTS ON AI ROI, EASING CONCERNS ON THE DURABILITY OF OPTICAL CAPEX, and while 800G has led growth, WE SEE THE 1.6T INFLECTION NOW UNDERWAY WITH A ~2x ASP UPLIFT."** ⚠️ **"The bigger overhang is SUPPLY, as a wave of C28 CAPACITY FOR SCALE-UP NPO/CPO RISKS AN OVERSHOOT THAT WOULD PRESSURE PRICING FIRST," plus InP tightness and the potential US ban on new Chinese transceivers — rated "BULLISH FOR COHR, LESS SO LITE."**

**(4) [[NVDA]]'s networking SVP speaks on CPO production and pluggables — pointer, not yet content.** DIGITIMES ran an interview with **Gilad Shainer, NVIDIA SVP of Networking**, on **"the progress of CPO PRODUCTION AND PLUGGABLE OPTICS,"** framed around **"building the key network architecture and optical interconnect technologies needed for AI factory deployments at scale"** (DIGITIMES, 2026-08-07). ⚠️ **Only the framing was captured — the substantive answers are behind the interview and are NOT on this page. Flagged for retrieval: NVIDIA's own view on the CPO-vs-pluggable timeline is the single most decision-relevant missing input for the [[LITE]]/[[COHR]]/[[TSEM]]/[[AAOI]] complex, and it is now on the record somewhere.** Same-day DIGITIMES headlines to pull with it: **"US OPTICAL TRANSCEIVER BAN RISKS SLOWING AI DATA CENTER BUILD-OUT"** (the ban's cost side, which this page has so far logged mainly as a Western-supplier benefit) and **"CHINESE OPTICAL SUPPLIERS CONFRONT FCC THREAT AFTER US$19.1B SELL-OFF."**

## Recent signals (Aug 5–6) — 🔴 the China dependency gets QUANTIFIED (56% of module manufacturing, EML/CW-laser share to 27.6% by 2028), the ban is scoped down to 2.4T+, and the sharpest new risk is a C28 CAPACITY OVERSHOOT — not cannibalisation

- 🔴 **THE STRUCTURAL WARNING OF THE WINDOW, and it inverts what the complex has been worrying about (Jefferies · Blayne Curtis, "[[COHR]]/[[LITE]] Previews — 1.6T Ramping, OCS Acceleration, Unpacking CPO/NPO Dynamics," 2026-08-05):** *"we would highlight **CAPACITY EXPANSION AS PERHAPS THE SINGLE GREATEST AREA OF CONCERN. THE RUBBER SEEMS TO MEET THE ROAD IN C28 WHERE A CONSIDERABLE AMOUNT OF CAPACITY COMES ONLINE INTENDED TO MEET DEMAND FOR SCALE-UP NPO/CPO, WHERE ROADMAPS HAVE YET TO BE FINALIZED. IF THESE RAMPS SLIP, THE INDUSTRY RISKS A CLASSIC CAPACITY OVERSHOOT INTO C28 AND THE PRICING GAINS THAT DROVE MARGIN EXPANSION WOULD BE THE FIRST CASUALTY.**"* ⚠️ **Read the structure: capacity is being committed against an architecture whose ROADMAP IS NOT YET FIXED. The risk is therefore a TIMING MISMATCH, not a demand miss, and the loss function is MARGIN. This is the mirror image of the CPO-cannibalisation fear this theme has tracked all quarter — the danger is not that CPO kills pluggables, it is that the industry builds for a scale-up CPO/NPO ramp that arrives late.**
- **✅ THE DEMAND/MIX DEBATE IS BEING TREATED AS SETTLED, in Barclays' words: "[[AMZN]] and [[MSFT]] earnings offered MATERIAL PROOF POINTS THAT AI IS GENERATING MEANINGFUL ROI, ALLEVIATING SOME INVESTOR CONCERNS AROUND DURABILITY OF CAPEX (I.E. OPTICAL) SPEND. Demand for 800G has been the primary growth driver thus far but WE EXPECT THE 1.6T INFLECTION IS NOW UNDERWAY OFFERING A MEANINGFUL ASP UPLIFT (~2X). Broadly speaking, CONCERNS ABOUT THE IMPACT OF CPO ON TRANSCEIVER DEMAND HAVE ACQUIESCED TO INVESTOR EXCITEMENT ON THE OPPORTUNITY FOR LASER TAM EXPANSION TIED TO NPO ROLLOUTS."** Corroborating from the switching side: **[[ANET]] guided 1.6T to "start TRIAL RAMP in 2H26 and MASS PRODUCTION IN 2027," with the 1.6T customer count "now at SINGLE DIGITS… but they are VERY LARGE customers," and sized the SCALE-ACROSS TAM at "US$15BN (US$20BN INCLUDING OPTICS) BY 2030 vs currently ~US$3–4BN"** (MS · Derrick Yang, Accton read-across from Arista's 2Q26, 2026-08-05).
- 🔴 **THE InP CHOKEPOINT IS TIGHTENING WITHIN THE QUARTER, AND NPO IS THE CAUSE: "LITE MANAGEMENT HIGHLIGHTED INCREMENTAL TIGHTNESS DEVELOPING INTRA-QUARTER RELATING TO INCREASED DEMAND FROM NPO. Outside of a few Japanese vendors (SUMITOMO ELECTRIC, JX), SUPPLY FOR InP CRYSTAL WAFER SUPPLY STEMS LARGELY FROM CHINA ([[AXTI]]) AND FALLS UNDER STRICT EXPORT CONTROLS"** (Barclays · Curtis, 2026-08-05). ⚠️ **This theme identified InP as the binding constraint on Jul 31 from the supplier side; it is now confirmed WORSENING from the customer side, and the cause is the very NPO ramp that constitutes the bull case. It also sits under Chinese export control — the SAME policy lever the US is proposing to pull on transceivers, i.e. symmetric escalation risk on the one component the Western complex cannot second-source quickly.**
- 🔴 **THE CHINA DEPENDENCY, FINALLY QUANTIFIED (TrendForce via @QQ_Timmy, 2026-08-06; translated inline):**
  - **China is ~56% OF GLOBAL OPTICAL-MODULE CONTRACT MANUFACTURING in 2026, of which export-oriented makers (Innolight, Eoptolink, Cambridge Technology) are ~46%.**
  - **"Global AI datacentres depend heavily on Chinese module manufacturing, PACKAGING/TEST AND InP LASER capacity; US customers CANNOT EXCLUDE Chinese manufacturing near-term, so supply gaps and cost pressure may rise and COULD EVEN SLOW AI DATACENTRE BUILDOUT."**
  - 🔴 **"CHINESE EML AND CW-LASER GLOBAL SHARE IS FORECAST TO RISE FROM 16.05% (2025) TO 27.58% (2028E) — but under US policy pressure MOST NEW CAPACITY CAN ONLY SERVE DOMESTIC DEMAND, INTENSIFYING PRICE COMPETITION AND OVERSUPPLY."**
  - **Scope caution from TrendForce itself: the draft is not public and "should NOT be read as a blanket ban at this stage" — "new model" definition, vendor criteria and transition periods are all undefined; policy may extend to CPO/NPO OPTICAL ENGINES and switches via FCC authorisation; the likely shape is "existing products continue, new models get heavier scrutiny."**
  - ⚠️ **THE SYNTHESIS THAT MATTERS: walling Chinese capacity into a domestic market does NOT shrink it — it makes it CHEAPER. That is Barclays' C28 overshoot arriving from the policy direction rather than the roadmap direction. The bull case for Western suppliers from the ban is real but bounded, and the bound is a price bound.**
- **SCOPE NARROWED: JPM's read is that the rule "seems ONLY TO APPLY TO 2.4T AND BEYOND DEVICES," which "shouldn't be a surprise given the DoD's Jun-26 addition of INNOLIGHT AND EOPTOLINK TO THE 1260H LIST"; with the optical datacom market "tracking to $50BN+," JPM sizes it as a "$10B OPPORTUNITY FOR US SUPPLIERS" (JPM · Joseph Cardoso via Joshua Meyers, 2026-08-05).** Against that, **Jefferies' Edison "basically thinks this ban is unlikely to materialize… we believe this is a US NEGOTIATION TACTIC AHEAD OF XI'S US VISIT IN SEP, and see a LOW RISK of this ban materializing under Trump," noting the FCC "does NOT classify datacenters as 'critical infrastructure' and NO Chinese optical module companies are included in the 'Covered Company List'" (Jefferies · Rankie Wong relaying Edison, 2026-08-05).** **Barclays' beneficiary ranking: "directionally bullish for [[COHR]] AND TO A LESSER EXTENT [[LITE]]," with merchant silicon ([[MRVL]]/[[AVGO]]) "BROADLY NEUTRAL over the long-term."** UBS's David Vogt is the outlier caution: a ban "WOULD NOT BE AS POSITIVE AS THE MARKET APPEARS TO BE SUGGESTING" (UBS TECH desk, 2026-08-05). **Chinese names gave back most of the initial move: Innolight −7%, Eoptolink −5% (Jefferies desk, 2026-08-05).**
- **⚠️ POSITIONING, and it names the crowded trade: from FMS — "a lot of people are LONG MEMORY SHORT OPTICS. Apparently ALL THE POINT72 PEOPLE THINK CHINA WILL FLOOD THE MARKET WITH LASERS" (@insane_analyst, 2026-08-06). Anecdotal, but it identifies the consensus expression of exactly the oversupply thesis above — worth knowing before treating that thesis as differentiated. Roundhill launched an OPTICAL NETWORKING ETF on 08-07 (@jukan05), a new price-insensitive marginal holder.**
- **NEW SCALE-UP DATAPOINT FROM [[NVDA]]'s side: the SOCAMM2/HBM de-spec is being offset architecturally — "SCALE-UP EXPANDING VIA NVL576 W/ NPO OPTICS TO OFFSET REDUCED MEMORY CONTENT" (GFHK via @jukan05, 2026-08-06).** ⚠️ **If correct, NPO optics content is being ADDED specifically because memory content was forced DOWN by supply — an optical-content tailwind sourced from a memory shortage. Unverified relay; logged as a mechanism to watch, and it is the most direct link yet between this theme and [themes/hbm-memory](hbm-memory.md).**
- **Adjacent: [[MRVL]]'s Photonic Fabric won the AI Infrastructure award at FMS 2026 — positioned as replacing electrical interconnect with optical I/O "across package-, server-, and rack-scale architectures" to enable "a DISAGGREGATED MEMORY ARCHITECTURE," on the argument that "memory bandwidth, memory locality, and data movement are pressing constraints on AI inference scalability and token efficiency" (@MarvellTech, 2026-08-05).** Company-sourced marketing, logged as a positioning datapoint only — but note it is the same disaggregation thesis [[MU]] gave DB at the same conference (HBM hot path / SOCAMM2 KV overflow / disaggregated DDR5 pools).

## Recent signals (Aug 4) — 🔴 THE US MOVES TO BAN NEW CHINESE OPTICAL TRANSCEIVERS, the whole Western optical complex rallies, and the desks immediately argue it is unimplementable

**The event.** Reuters reported the **Trump administration is drafting a restriction on US imports of NEW-MODEL Chinese optical transceivers and datacenter components** — per MS's desk, being drafted at the **FCC**; per @zerohedge, "four people familiar with the matter." Structure as reported: **"the agency would ban all imports of new transceiver models and then EXEMPT MANY NON-CHINESE SUPPLIERS from the restrictions"** (@aleabitoreddit, 2026-08-04). Targets would be **China's Innolight, Eoptolink** and the broader Chinese optical-interconnect chain.

**The tape.** **[[COHR]] +12%, [[LITE]] +9%**, with **[[GLW]], [[CIEN]], VIAV, [[AAOI]], FN, LWLG and [[POET]] all rallying** (MS TMT desk · Tom Wigg, 2026-08-04; TMTB EOD Wrap, 2026-08-04). @aleabitoreddit called it **"a very material catalyst… for Western supply chains from $AAOI, $SIVE/Jabil, to $LITE and Coherent."**

**MS's read, which is the most useful because it splits winners from mechanics (MS Research via desk, 2026-08-04):**
- **[[COHR]] is "the CLEAREST SCALED BENEFICIARY given its VERTICAL INTEGRATION"**; **[[AAOI]] and FN "also positioned to absorb incremental demand."**
- **[[LITE]] "should benefit from TIGHTER EML SUPPLY but faces potential InP SUBSTRATE RISK FROM CHINA"** — the two-sided position.
- ⚠️ **"The bigger issue is FEASIBILITY: non-Chinese suppliers CANNOT CURRENTLY SUPPORT TOTAL AI DEMAND, making access to Chinese InP supply critical," noting clouds have spent YEARS qualifying alternatives — and MS believes "a broader US-China agreement involving RECIPROCAL COMPONENT PURCHASES could ultimately be part of the solution."**

**The pushback, and it was immediate and near-unanimous.** MS desk: *"Early investor pushback is that there's just NOT ENOUGH OPTICAL CAPACITY to serve the US Data Center market without using China components, so this is likely MORE SABRE RATTLING WITH IMPLEMENTATION UNLIKELY IN THE MEDIUM TERM."* TMTB: *"Lots of pushback and confusion about what exactly this means and how and if it gets implemented."*

⚠️ **THE THREE UNRESOLVED QUESTIONS that determine whether this is tradeable — carried open rather than netted:**
1. **Is manufacturing IN China covered, or only corporate domicile?** TMTB: *"Key question is also whether mfg in China is part of the ban or JUST A CORPORATE DOMICILED BAN, which would affect how much it impacts LITE and COHR and others."* This is the single biggest swing factor, because several "Western" suppliers manufacture in China.
2. **What is the retaliation?** MS: *"China has an obvious retaliatory mechanism by possibly restricting bottlenecked InP (Indium Phosphide) supply via [[AXTI]] (Chinese)."* ⚠️ **Note [[LITE]] "just agreed to terms with AXTI re InP supply"** — so the InP dependency is fresh and contractual. @jukan05 went further: *"Will China still allow InP exports to the U.S.? I'M VERY BEARISH ON AXTI"* (2026-08-04). Cross-reference the standing InP-as-binding-constraint thread (Jul 31) — **this is the same chokepoint, now with a political trigger attached.**
3. **US suppliers sell INTO the Chinese optical players as well as competing with them.** MS: *"a lot of the US companies such as [[LITE]], [[MRVL]], [[AVGO]] & [[NVDA]] SELL INTO the Chinese optical players as well as competing, so this creates DISRUPTION EVEN IF SHARE SHIFTS."* The gross-vs-net effect on US names is genuinely ambiguous.

**Adjacent capacity signal:** **Furukawa (5801 JP) announced an expansion to DOUBLE optical fiber and optical-fiber-cable capacity across four countries** (MS Research, 2026-08-04).

**How to hold this:** the *direction* (bifurcation of optical supply chains, greater strategic value on Western players) is consistent with everything else on this page; the *magnitude and timing* are not underwritten by anyone, and the feasibility objection is a capacity fact rather than a political opinion. **Treat the 9-12% moves as a re-rating of optionality, not of earnings, until the scope question (#1) is answered.** See [[COHR]] · [[LITE]] · [[AAOI]] · [[CIEN]] · [[AXTI]] · [themes/china-export](china-export.md).

## Recent signals (Aug 3) — ★ [[NVDA]] says CPO IS IN MASS PRODUCTION, Morgan Stanley says the near-term architecture is NPO, and both are probably right

**★ ① THE COMPANY CLAIM, AND IT IS THE STRONGEST CPO STATEMENT ON THIS PAGE: "Nvidia Senior VP GILAD SHAINER said CO-PACKAGED OPTICS (CPO) HAS ENTERED MASS PRODUCTION to meet the massive data throughput and speeds required by Nvidia's VERA RUBIN platform, which is AT LEAST 3x FASTER THAN GB300," adding that the bandwidth performance of scale-up (intra-rack/chip interconnection) "will be 10x THAT OF SCALE OUT" (@dnystedt, 2026-08-03, 112 likes).** ⚠️ **Scope it narrowly before treating the CPO-timing debate as closed. (a) It is a platform-generation claim about Vera Rubin, not a statement that a finalised Rubin Ultra rack exists — Fubon the SAME DAY says the next-gen rack "design [is] NOT YET COMPLETED" and "we are already in Aug, so the TIMELINE IS VERY TIGHT to enter Rubin Ultra production." (b) This page has carried "CPO NVSwitch only at Feynman" from SemiAnalysis (07-06) and MS's markdown of 2028 CPO scale; a CPO-in-MP claim on the Rubin platform is a different assertion from CPO in the scale-up switch domain, and the two should not be netted. (c) It is a media relay of an executive remark, not a disclosure.**

**★ ② THE MORE CONSEQUENTIAL CALL, AND IT CUTS THE OTHER WAY: the commercial emphasis is shifting from CPO to NPO (Morgan Stanley · Meta Marshall / Antonio Jaramillo, "[[LITE]]/[[COHR]] FQ4 Preview", 2026-08-03).** **Verbatim: "Expect NPO to receive GREATER STRATEGIC EMPHASIS this quarter as THE NEXT OPTICAL ARCHITECTURE APPROACHING COMMERCIAL VIABILITY… management commentary is likely to place relatively greater emphasis on NEAR-PACKAGE OPTICS (NPO) VERSUS CPO, given NPO's positioning as a MORE SERVICEABLE AND LESS COMPLEX step toward moving optics closer to the XPU. NPO also appears capable of attracting a BROADER CUSTOMER BASE, PARTICULARLY AMONG NON-NVIDIA PLATFORMS seeking improved power efficiency WITHOUT IMMEDIATELY ADOPTING A FULLY CO-PACKAGED DESIGN. CPO remains an important longer-term opportunity, but NPO INCREASINGLY LOOKS LIKE THE MORE TANGIBLE BRIDGE between today's pluggables and eventual co-packaged architectures."**
- **THE RISK IS RESTATED PRECISELY, AND IT IS NOT TIMING: "While we DON'T SEE DELAYS TO CPO TIMING, as mentioned in our deep dive, OUR CONCERN IS MORE AROUND DEGREE OF ADOPTION with NPO SEEMING TO GAIN MORE TRACTION."** ⚠️ **That is the reconciliation between ① and ②, and it is the framing this page should adopt: CPO can be in mass production on the NVIDIA platform while NPO wins the broader non-NVIDIA base. The bull case for the optics complex has always rested partly on CPO content per port; if the volume architecture is NPO, the content step-up is smaller even though the vendors are the same. MS's offset: "much of the underlying LASER AND OPTICAL-COMPONENT STACK IS SHARED between NPO and CPO," so LITE and COHR are positioned either way — the mix question is about content, not about who wins.**
- **Near-term revenue stays with pluggables regardless: "Pluggable transceivers and the associated laser, photonic and passive-component content should remain the PRIMARY NEAR-TERM GROWTH DRIVERS for LITE and COHR, supported by continued 800G ADOPTION and the RAMP OF 1.6T. Near-term results should therefore remain MORE TIED TO CONVENTIONAL PLUGGABLE CAPACITY, YIELDS AND CUSTOMER DEPLOYMENTS than to revenue from emerging architectures."** **MS does not expect the FQ4 prints to resolve any of it — "OCP LIKELY GREATER POSITIVE CATALYST."** MS keeps **EW on both, PT $900 LITE / $330 COHR**, and prefers **COHR** into the print on gross-margin upside. Valuation context: **LITE/COHR now ~20x FY28 P/E vs 25x buyside CY28 earlier in the quarter.** Named risks: **a broader AI valuation correction, slower capacity execution, and continued EML constraints.**

**★ ③ THE BOTTLENECK MOVES DOWNSTREAM — [[AXTI]] substrate easing, internal laser fab now the constraint (same MS note).** **"AXTI suggests the InP SUBSTRATE BOTTLENECK IS BEGINNING TO EASE, leaving INTERNAL FAB CAPACITY AS THE MORE IMPORTANT CONSTRAINT… Improved substrate visibility should help both companies, but INTERNAL InP FAB CAPACITY increasingly appears to be THE PRIMARY BOTTLENECK on incremental output."** ⚠️ **This is a direct revision of the framing this page adopted on Jul 31 ("the InP substrate layer IS the binding constraint, and Lumentum's own behaviour proves it"). Both entries stay. The consequence is about WHERE THE SCARCITY RENT SITS: if substrate stops being scarce, pricing power migrates from AXTI to whoever owns the laser fab — a margin-mix question rather than a volume question.** **Supporting detail: AXTI on track to DOUBLE InP substrate capacity in 2026 and expand slightly beyond in 2027; China export permits "becoming MORE REGULAR, although applications still require a supporting customer order and take time to process"; supply still tight enough that "customers are using LEGACY 2-INCH WAFERS alongside mainstream 3-inch and 4-inch formats, while 6-INCH WAFERS ARE PROGRESSING BUT HAVE NOT REACHED VOLUME PRODUCTION." The two LTAs serve different purposes: "LITE's long-term supply agreement RESERVES SUBSTRATE CAPACITY THROUGH 2031, while COHR's agreement supports the DEVELOPMENT AND SUPPLY OF 6-INCH WAFERS."**

**④ Fubon's read on NVDA's optical intent is unchanged and is the one datapoint both sides agree on: "nVidia is still VERY AGGRESSIVE on NPO/CPO SOLUTIONS," with the two-rack "Taycan" candidate using NPO/CPO for the scale-out connection BETWEEN racks and cable retained for scale-up inside (Jefferies/Fubon · Sherman Shang, 2026-08-03).** ⚠️ **Note the architectural placement: Fubon has NPO/CPO doing the INTER-RACK scale-out job. That is consistent with MS's NPO emphasis and with Shainer's mass-production claim, and it is a different claim from CPO in the scale-up switch — which remains the Feynman-era item.**

**⑤ TAM datapoint from the corpus, logged as directional rather than as an estimate: "CW LASERS projected to become a ~$10 BILLION MARKET… moving from $1B in 2026 to $10B by 2030," with [[LITE]], [[COHR]], [[AAOI]], SIVE and MTSI named as the ways to play it (@crux_capital_, 2026-08-03, 107 likes).** Promotional FinTwit source, no methodology disclosed.

**★ ⑥ THE PRIMARY TRANSCRIPT BEHIND ④, AND IT IS THE MOST DECISION-RELEVANT CPO ITEM IN THE CALL: NPO/CPO IS NO LONGER A ROADMAP LINE, IT IS ONE OF TWO LIVE RACK ARCHITECTURES COMPETING FOR THE RUBIN ULTRA SLOT — WITH A HARD DEADLINE (Fubon · Sherman Shang / Arthur (hosted by Jefferies), "industry highlights & checks update", 2026-08-03 — machine transcript; per this run's ASR glossary `Titan`←"Tycan"/"TaiWan", `Kyber`←"Kyper", `Fubon`←"Foobon", so the "Taycan" in ④ above and the "Titan" here are the same candidate design).**
- **THE CHOICE, STATED AS A CHOICE: "following Nvidia's cancellation or push out of the Kyber rack and the four-compute Rubin Ultra GPU… Nvidia is currently working on a few new rack architectures. WE FIND THAT NVIDIA STILL PLANS TO PUT THE SAME TOTAL OF 576 COMPUTE DIES inside the new rack architecture or system. Currently, we see TWO DIFFERENT DESIGNS. The FIRST is a MORE CONSERVATIVE design that uses FOUR EXISTING "BEER ONE" [ASR-garbled rack name] RACKS in a single system connected via TRADITIONAL SCALE-OUT CONNECTION. The SECOND architecture we are seeing is a TWO-RACK DESIGN, AKA TITAN, THAT USES NPO OR CPO FOR SCALE-OUT CONNECTIONS BETWEEN THE TWO RACKS."** ⚠️ **This is the framing this page should adopt for the next two quarters, because it converts an adoption-rate debate into a binary design decision with a dated resolution. The compute content is FIXED at 576 dies either way — so the optics content per system is the whole difference between the two candidates. A four-rack traditional scale-out build is pluggables; a two-rack Titan build is NPO/CPO between racks. Same silicon, materially different optics BOM.**
- **THE INTENT CHECK, WHICH IS THE ANSWER TO ② ABOVE: "DESPITE A LOT OF MARKET NOISE ON CPO, based on our checks, we think NVIDIA IS STILL VERY AGGRESSIVE REGARDING THE NPO AND THE CPO SOLUTION."** ⚠️ **Weight it for what it is: a supply-chain read on INTENT, not a design win. The same paragraph withholds the confirmation — "the rack design is NOT YET COMPLETED… we currently have limited information on the spec details."**
- **★ THE DEADLINE, AND IT IS THE SHARPEST TIMING STATEMENT ON THIS PAGE: "we want to highlight that, you know, WE ARE ALREADY IN AUGUST. SO THE TIMELINE IS VERY, VERY TIGHT TO ENTER PRODUCTION FOR RUBIN ULTRA."** **The dated sequence around it (Arthur, from Taiwan ODM visits): "Rubin rack will be start to shipping on the this year DECEMBER QUARTER. And the NEW RACK DESIGN FOR RUBIN ULTRA probably will be DECEMBER QUARTER OF 2027," with downstream clarity "probably will be first quarter or a little later, second quarter for 2027… at least BEFORE THE SECOND QUARTER OF 2027." Hon Hai has put "several solution… a lot of proposal, two rack, four rack" to Nvidia.** ⚠️ **So the decision window for a Dec-2027-quarter rack is roughly the next two to three quarters, and the tell will show up in the ODM/optics order book before it shows up in a spec sheet. That makes 1Q-2Q27 the checkable date for this page's central question, and it is EARLIER than the OCP catalyst MS points to in ② above.**
- **WHY THE OPTICS QUESTION EXISTS AT ALL — the physical constraint, from the ODM side (Arthur): Nvidia's fixed goal is "TO PUT THE 144 GPU PER RACK, THAT'S FOR SURE. And the main bottleneck is faced the HEAT DISSIPATION, CABLE DESIGN, and the fact of some POWER DENSITY OF THE EXISTING DATA CENTER CANNOT SUPPORT IT, because currently, as you know, ALL THE GPU CONNECT ON THE RACK IS STILL USED THE CABLE. So this is very complex. So a single rack potentially the power will be 1 MILLION WATT."** ⚠️ **This is the mechanism the CPO thesis has always needed and rarely gets stated so plainly by a supply-chain source: copper cabling is one of the three named blockers to 144 GPUs in a rack, alongside thermals and site power. NPO/CPO is being evaluated because the cable plant does not scale to the density Nvidia has already committed to — which is a stronger form of the adoption argument than any TAM estimate on this page. Cross-file [ai-datacenter-power](ai-datacenter-power.md) and [800v-dc-power](800v-dc-power.md) for the ~1MW-per-rack figure; [cowos-packaging](cowos-packaging.md) for the 576-compute-die packaging load; [outros-asia](outros-asia.md) for Hon Hai.** [Source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_08_03_sherman_update_03_08_26.html)

## Recent signals (Aug 2) — the CPO enabler (SoIC) gets its first capacity CUT while the desk closest to it says adoption will be slower, and the July optics unwind is shown to be positioning rather than demand

**★ ① THE PHYSICAL ENABLER OF CPO WAS JUST REVISED DOWN, AND THE HOUSE CUTTING IT IS EXPLICITLY SCEPTICAL ON CPO TIMING (Fubon, Jefferies' Taiwan affiliate, via William Beavington's Global SEMIS Daily, 2026-08-02).** **"Our affiliate Fubon CUT ESTIMATES ON TSMC's SoIC EXPANSION, with its SoIC capacity now expected to rise to 70K WPM BY Q4-28 VS 90K PREVIOUSLY. SoIC capacity is estimated at 35K WPM [today]. TSMC's CoWoS capacity CONTINUES TO BE FORECAST to rise from 120K WPM in Q4-26 to 220K WPM by Q4-28, WITH POTENTIAL UPSIDE."** **Fubon names TSMC's SoIC customers as [[AMD]] and [[AAPL]], "with CPO FOR OPTICAL CONNECTIVITY A KEY LONGER-TERM DRIVER" — and the desk relaying it adds its own view: "HOWEVER, WE REMAIN MORE CONSERVATIVE IN OUR EXPECTATION FOR RATE OF CPO ADOPTION IN THE INDUSTRY."**
- ⚠️ **THIS IS THE FIRST DOWNWARD REVISION TO CPO-ENABLING CAPACITY THAT THIS THEME HAS LOGGED, AND THE ASYMMETRY MATTERS: 3D STACKING (SoIC) cut −22% for 2028 while 2.5D INTERPOSER (CoWoS) held with upside. Co-packaged optics needs the stacking capacity; the pluggable/near-package world does not. A −22% cut to the 2028 SoIC ceiling, published by a house that also thinks CPO adoption will be slower than consensus, pushes the CPO content step-up beyond the current forecast window.**
- **HOW TO READ IT FOR THE MODULE VENDORS, and it is genuinely two-sided rather than a straightforward negative: slower CPO EXTENDS THE PLUGGABLE RUNWAY at 800G/1.6T, which is where [[LITE]], [[COHR]] and [[AAOI]] have established content and where the demand/supply gap is currently binding. It simultaneously DEFERS the CPO laser-content step-up that the longer-dated bull case needs. This page should not treat CPO delay as unambiguously bad OR good — it changes the timing of the mix shift, not the direction.** **Tool-level read-throughs: negative for [[BESI]] (hybrid bonders qualified into SoIC); "any upside to CoWoS/CoPoS capacity expansion will be POSITIVE FOR SUSS, which supplies UV SCANNERS to TSMC for these processes."** ⚠️ **Note the standing counter-datapoint on this page from [[MEDIATEK]] management (07-31): co-packaged COPPER (CPC) today with CPO via TSMC COUPE beyond it, and "beyond 400G SerDes will need to move to OPTICAL" — with 448G SerDes ready 2H27. So the architectural necessity of optical is not in question; the CAPACITY to package it at volume by 2028 just got marked down. See [themes/cowos-packaging](cowos-packaging.md), [[TSM]].**

**★ ② THE JULY OPTICS DRAWDOWN IS SHOWN TO BE A POSITIONING EVENT — WITH THE FUNDAMENTAL QUESTION EXPLICITLY LEFT OPEN (Gaetano, "Why Semiconductors and Optics Fell Apart", Part 1, 2026-08-02).** **"The Semiconductor and Optics trade NEEDED NO SUDDEN COLLAPSE IN END DEMAND to fall this hard. It only need[ed] TOO MANY INVESTORS TO OWN THE SAME FUTURE, FINANCED WITH TOO MUCH LEVERAGE." Tracked optical names fell 30-70% IN SIX WEEKS while SOXX stayed +67.7% YTD.**
- ⚠️ **THE CROWDING MECHANISM EXPLAINS WHY OPTICS TRADED IDENTICALLY TO MEMORY AND POWER DESPITE SELLING UNRELATED PRODUCTS: "The stocks owned were DIVERSIFIED ON PAPER. Like one fund having major exposure to memory, another to optical, another to power and so on. But ALL OF THESE POSITIONS RELY ON THE SAME UNDERLYING ASSUMPTION that hyperscalers will continue expanding AI infra at an exceptional pace for years, WITH AN ROI THAT JUSTIFIES THE EXPENSE. When that common assumption is challenged… THE BASKET BEHAVES LIKE ONE TRADE." For this theme specifically, that means the July move carries almost NO information about transceiver demand, attach rates or the CPO transition — which is exactly the inference many desks drew from it.**
- **The flow evidence, which pre-dates the blamed catalysts: US hedge funds sold tech hardware for a fourth consecutive week by early July; US semi funds saw ~$11BN OF OUTFLOWS IN THE WEEK ENDED JUNE 24 — "THE LARGEST WEEKLY WITHDRAWAL THIS CENTURY" — after ~$12bn of inflows the prior two weeks; Asia-focused fundamental L/S funds −18.6% on average through 28 July; record de-grossing in the final week; Goldman's High Beta Momentum Basket posted its worst month since 2000.** ⚠️ **"So much of this began BEFORE Kimi, before the accelerated Kospi crash, and before the Leo / SA Fund drama. All of these ACCELERATED A PROCESS THAT WAS ALREADY UNDERWAY."**
- ⚠️ **WHAT IS EXPLICITLY NOT ESTABLISHED: this is Part 1 of a series and covers market structure only — "THE NEXT REPORT WILL EXAMINE WHETHER THE UNDERLYING OPTICS BUSINESSES WEAKENED." It shows the price move did not REQUIRE demand deterioration, not that demand held. Paid post; the author is long the theme. Flagged as a follow-up to ingest when Part 2 publishes — it is the piece that would actually bear on this theme's fundamentals.**
- **Recovery marker: 22V's Jordi Visser named [[AAOI]] alongside [[MU]], [[MRVL]] and [[GLW]] as "rubble" names already back above rising 200-day moving averages (2026-08-02).**

**③ A single-name pre-print datapoint that sets a falsifiable manufacturing bar for the whole capacity-constrained thesis.** **On [[AAOI]] into its Q2 print: "the ONLY WESTERN VERTICALLY INTEGRATED TRANSCEIVER MAKER WITH ITS OWN InP LASER FAB pointed directly at the 800G/1.6T ramp… IF THE CAPACITY LANDS, THE REVENUE IS ESSENTIALLY PRE-SOLD due to the demand/supply gap. Everything else, like a high multiple / dilution risk / CPO TIMELINES, IS SECONDARY NOISE to whether $AAOI can actually build modules fast enough." Management's own Q1 statement: "THE ACTUAL DEMAND IS NOT $1.1B, IT IS $1.4-1.5B," with the FY guide raised only to "exceed $1.1B" because "PRODUCTION AND SUPPLY CHAINS ARE THEIR CAP RIGHT NOW." The ramp to score: 800G/1.6T capacity from ~100,000 MODULES/MONTH IN Q1 to >650,000/MONTH BY YEAR-END and ~930,000/MONTH thereafter** (@ParadisLabs, 2026-08-01, 806 likes; self-declared long, numbers are the author's construction). ⚠️ **Useful to this theme regardless of the name: it converts "the industry is supply-constrained" into a checkable monthly-unit trajectory at one vendor, and it locates the constraint at MODULE ASSEMBLY rather than at lasers or substrates — which is a different bottleneck from the InP-substrate constraint this page identified on 07-31. Both can bind; watch which one the Q2 prints blame.**

## Recent signals (Aug 1, PRIMARY SOURCE) — the CPO test TAM gets sized and dated by the vendor, and 2027 is guided to the LOW end
_⚠️ Recovery ingest: the [[TER]] Q2 CY26 call transcript (2026-07-29) was archived unrouted on 07-31 and restored on 08-01. Bloomberg FINAL TRANSCRIPT._
- **★ CPO TEST TAM: $300-700M BY 2028 — AND MANAGEMENT GUIDES 2027 TO THE BOTTOM OF THE IMPLIED PATH.** Teradyne: *"we expect **CPO alone will be a $300 million to $700 million market by 2028**."* Asked by GS why the range is so wide: *"that's our attempt to be as honest as we can be. Like we really are looking at a number of different market sources in terms of the number of CPO ports that are going to be produced, and **they're all over the map**."* The discriminator is explicit — ***"it really comes down to how quickly the initial higher volume ramps of CPO, primarily for scale out, achieve success over the 2027 timeframe. That's what's going to drive that 2028 TAM"*** toward $700M rather than $300M. ⚠️ **The 2027 mark is the news, and it is a downgrade relative to how this theme has been carrying the ramp: *"Next year, I think we're probably like — probably aiming towards more of the low side of that $300 million. So if you draw a line from the $100 million to the $300 million, next year, kind of the low-end would be in the $200 million range. **I don't think there is as much upside next year as there is upside in 2028**."*** So: ~$100M (2026 implied) → ~$200M (2027, low end) → $300-700M (2028) (Teradyne Q2 CY26 call, 2026-07-29).
- **★ THE "CAN INSERTION 2 BE SKIPPED?" DEBATE GETS A TECHNICAL ANSWER, AND IT IS NO.** TD Cowen put the industry view directly — Advantest strong in insertion 1, TER in insertion 2, and *"there is some view in the industry that **insertion 2 can be skipped** if you have known good die."* Greg Smith's rebuttal is the most specific process explanation logged on this theme: ***"getting a known good die at insertion 1 doesn't help you all that much in terms of the quality of the optical engine"*** — because between the two insertions you **bond the electronic IC to the photonic IC wafer and then apply a polymer lens**, and *"**the lens assembly is a critical part of the optical path. So many of the loss and polarization tests that you need to do can't be done until you have the completed wafer**… **you can't do everything that you need to do to validate the quality of the device at insertion 1**."* ⚠️ **The genuine open question he concedes is narrower than "skip or not": *"how much of that testing needs to happen at the wafer level post-bonding and how much of it can be deferred"* to assembled optical engines.** ⚠️ **And a caution that should temper every insertion-share claim on this page, including the [[ADVANTEST]] and Chroma ones: *"we were a good 15 minutes into the conversation before we discovered that we and the customer had an entirely different definition of what insertion 2 was… it's so early in the maturation of that production process that I think it's a mistake to draw broad conclusions"*** (Teradyne Q2 CY26 call, 2026-07-29).
- **⚠️ THE VENDOR IS DELIBERATELY HEDGED ACROSS THE PHYSICAL LAYER, WHICH IS ITSELF A STATEMENT ABOUT CPO TIMING RISK.** *"While the total number of network connections is growing fast, **the timing of transitions from cable to backplane and from pluggable to XPO and CPO is in constant flux**."* Hence three separate vehicles: **Quantifi Photonics** (acquisition), the **Photon 100** solution for optical connections, and the **MultiLane Test Products (MLTP) JV** for copper. *"Pluggables are still growing and will transition, **not just to CPO. There's NPO and XPO stages along that path**… Teradyne's strategy is really to make sure that we're positioned to benefit from the growth in all of those segments."* **Read that as a supplier declining to underwrite a clean pluggable→CPO transition — consistent with the SemiAnalysis CPO skepticism this theme already carries.** The hedge underneath it all: *"the networking silicon, the switch silicon, and **Teradyne has a terrific position in that market that we think will grow over the mid-term independent of the physical layer**"* (Teradyne Q2 CY26 call, 2026-07-29).
- **Ecosystem structure, and why no single vendor locks the CPO test stack.** *"The best example of that ecosystem developing is really in **the whole silicon photonic space**. That it's early days where there's **individual partnerships sort of against the initial customer ramps**, but ultimately, that's going to turn into the same **open ecosystem where each test company works with all of the suppliers of material handling and vice versa**."* TER says it is *"working with multiple ecosystem partners to develop leading-edge solutions **from silicon photonics wafers to full data center racks**."* ⚠️ **Practical implication for the share fight logged on this theme (TER / [[ADVANTEST]] / FormFactor / Keysight / Chroma): early insertion-share claims are being made against pilot-customer definitions, in a stack that management expects to become open and multi-vendor** (Teradyne Q2 CY26 call, 2026-07-29).

## Recent signals (Aug 1) — the constraint moves one layer further upstream (tools), and a TFLN foundry raises money to attack the platform itself
- **★ THE CONSTRAINT IS NOT ONLY SUBSTRATE — IT IS ALSO THE DEPOSITION TOOL. Reading [[AIXA]]'s print against [[AXTI]]'s: "AIXA saying that their DEMAND OUTSTRIPS THEIR SUPPLY and that CUSTOMERS ARE RESERVING SLOTS INTO NEXT YEAR means that their customers are SHORT MOCVD MACHINES relative to what their demand is"** (@crux_capital_, 2026-07-31). ⚠️ **This theme established on 07-31 that InP SUBSTRATE availability — not cost, not module capacity — is the binding constraint, with [[LITE]]'s [[AXTI]] LTA (signed despite a ~7-year Sumitomo deal) as the proof. The AIXA read adds a second, independent chokepoint one step downstream of the substrate and upstream of the laser: even a laser maker holding substrate cannot necessarily grow epi on it, because MOCVD tool slots are booked into 2027. Two serial constraints compound — they do not substitute — so the supply response to the 800G/1.6T pull is slower than either layer alone implies, and the pricing window for whoever holds capacity today is longer.** The author's downstream inference — that constrained laser makers outsource epitaxy to IQE, which "has a ton of MOCVD machines," citing MTSI and [[TSEM]] as proof the model works — **is a hypothesis, not a disclosed arrangement**; he explicitly wants "more formal agreements" and flags specification/qualification risk. Logged to test.
- **A NEW PLATFORM ENTRANT AT THE FOUNDRY LAYER — thin-film lithium niobate (TFLN) gets a dedicated pure-play: Switzerland's CCRAFT, a one-year-old CSEM spinout, raised US$7.8M (CHF 6.3M) led by QBIT Capital, with Zürcher Kantonalbank, Apprecia Capital, Spacewalk, Blue Wonder Ventures and — notably — "A LEADING EUROPEAN AI INFRASTRUCTURE OPERATOR" participating** (Semiecosystem · Mark LaPedus, 2026-08-01). **The technical claim, which is a vendor claim and should be carried as one: TFLN chips promise "UP TO 8X HIGHER SPEEDS and consume UP TO 10X LESS ENERGY than conventional optical components," on the argument that "most existing optoelectronic platforms, such as SILICON PHOTONICS or INDIUM PHOSPHIDE (InP), CANNOT DELIVER THE LEAP IN PERFORMANCE THE MARKET NEEDS." Founder/CEO Hamed Sattari: TFLN combines "high electro-optic efficiency, low optical losses, a wide transparency window, optical nonlinearities, and compatibility with microelectronic systems," with both MONOLITHIC (all-TFLN) and HYBRID (TFLN + silicon) designs offered.** ⚠️ **Sizing discipline: US$7.8M is seed-scale against a market where [[TSEM]] alone discloses $1.3bn of SiPho orders and [[AXTI]]/[[LITE]] are signing multi-year InP LTAs. CCRAFT is NOT a competitive threat to anything on this page on any relevant horizon, and it gets no company page (off-coverage, no local archive). It is logged for exactly one reason: it is the first evidence in this theme's flow of capital forming around a THIRD material platform positioned explicitly against BOTH incumbents — silicon photonics and InP — at the foundry layer rather than the module layer. The InP-scarcity thesis that currently drives [[AXTI]], [[LITE]], [[COHR]] and [[AAOI]] on this page is a bet that no adequate substitute arrives before the capacity does; TFLN is the substitute candidate to keep a watch on, and the participation of an AI-infrastructure operator as an investor is the tell worth tracking rather than the round size.**

## Recent signals (Jul 31) — ★ the InP substrate layer is the binding constraint, and Lumentum's own behaviour proves it
- **★ THE CLEANEST EVIDENCE YET THAT SUBSTRATE AVAILABILITY — NOT COST, NOT MODULE CAPACITY — IS THE BINDING CONSTRAINT: [[LITE]] has signed a long-term InP substrate supply agreement with [[AXTI]], despite having, only months earlier, expressed confidence in a ~7-YEAR SUMITOMO AGREEMENT. The analyst's framing is the tell: "just a few months ago Lumentum felt confident in their long term supply agreement with SUMITOMO, covering 7 years… This was ONE OF THE BIGGEST REASONS WHY I WAS BEARISH ON AXT. They didn't really feel necessary to me. So the fact that just a few months later Lumentum is signing LTAs with AXT really shows JUST HOW FAST THINGS PROGRESS AND HOW SIGNIFICANT THE DEMAND IS." The Lumentum CEO has been "open recently about how they need to MULTI SOURCE AGGRESSIVELY and secure as much as possible"** (AXT Q2 2026 earnings call, primary, via @Gaetano buyside notes, 2026-07-30/31). ⚠️ **A 7-year incumbent agreement proved insufficient. The inference: substrate COST is not the concern — AVAILABILITY is.**
- **The money is moving upstream, which prices the scarcity: AXT disclosed prepayments/LTA cash of $25.4M FROM [[COHR]] and $22.3M FROM CASELA, plus a Lumentum agreement reserving minimum annual InP capacity through 2031 with an initial $43.5M deposit and a further $43.5M deposit.** ⚠️ **ACCOUNTING TRAP, flagged because it is the most likely misread: this cash is "posted as a LIABILITY and… converted to revenue as they ship against the agreements. So keep in mind these DIDN'T SHOW UP IN THE REV NUMBERS THIS Q." Bridging LTA announcements to reported revenue double-counts.**
- **The supply ramp, and it is running ahead of plan: AXT is AHEAD OF SCHEDULE on doubling InP capacity in 2026, with the InP revenue opportunity "on track to MORE THAN TRIPLE by the end of 2026" and continued significant expansion in 2027. Backlog >$100M. ~$66M of Q3 revenue realizable TODAY (no export permit required). ~$130M/QUARTER exiting 2027 (~$520M annualised at the low end). Post-expansion AXT expects to be "BY FAR THE LARGEST INDIUM PHOSPHIDE PRODUCER IN THE WORLD."** Drivers include **NEW CRYSTAL GROWTH FURNACE DESIGNS** and customers **moving to LARGER-DIAMETER (6-INCH) substrates and higher-value products.**
- **And it is still not enough: "DEMAND OUTPACES SUPPLY, NO MATTER HOW FAST THEY ADD CAPACITY… AXT is shipping RECORD LEVELS of InP wafers to companies like LITE and COHR. AND THEY STILL WANT MORE. And LITE and COHR's customers want more. EVERYONE WANTS MORE."**
- **The demand mechanism, stated three ways and worth keeping precise: (1) the 800G→1.6T transition is OVERLAPPING demand, not substitution; (2) MORE LASERS PER TRANSCEIVER; (3) higher-spec/more valuable substrates. Plus "HYPERSCALERS ARE ADVANCING TOWARD NPO AND CPO WHICH WILL CONTINUE INCREASING DEMAND."** ⚠️ **Also: customers buying gallium arsenide are "also very interested in InP" — VCSEL and InP demand curves move TOGETHER rather than competing, consistent with both COHR and LITE carrying both product lines.**
- **⚠️ Geography and the unforecast upside: China InP revenue DOUBLED Q/Q in Q2 and is "not gated by permits… just straight capacity ramp + demand," at ~40-60% of AXT's InP revenue — leaving substantial ex-China headroom. AXT STILL LACKS PERMITS TO SHIP INTO THE US: "this is still without permits to ship into the U.S. If that unlocks, you are looking at A MAJOR STEP UP AGAIN THAT ISN'T BEING FORECASTED." Some easing noted — "more regularity in the process of permits in certain geographic regions" (in a Coherent context, so likely Germany/Sweden/Switzerland).** Tape: **AXTI +13% intraday 07-30 then >25% MORE after hours.**
