- **NEW (2026-07-17) — MS sizes the scale-up networking TAM at ~$73B and refreshes the CPO-vs-copper timeline; KEYS upgraded to OW (Morgan Stanley · Meta Marshall / Ella Tulchinsky, Tech Talk Ep. 316 + "Scale-Up Primer," 2026-07-13/17).** In an MS Tech Talk podcast (companion to the Jul-13 "Bringing More Light to Scale-Up Networks" primer), Marshall/Tulchinsky put a **new scale-up networking TAM of ~$73B** on the copper→optics transition, walk the **CPO timeline** (copper ramps first; CPO the later leg), and flag the **copper ramp + ASIC beneficiaries AVGO / ALAB / MRVL** alongside stock views on **LITE / COHR / GLW**. MS also **upgraded Keysight (KEYS) to Overweight (Jul-13)** on diversification across AI architectures (test/measurement pick-and-shovel). Adds a fresh TAM anchor to the page's supply-gated up-cycle thesis. (Morgan Stanley · Meta Marshall / Ella Tulchinsky, 2026-07-13/17; see [../COHR.md](../COHR.md), [../GLW.md](../GLW.md), [../AVGO.md](../AVGO.md), [../ALAB.md](../ALAB.md), [../MRVL.md](../MRVL.md))
- **GAP-FILL (added 2026-08-10, from the PRIMARY 39-page note behind the bullet above) — the XPU-BY-XPU optical adoption calendar, which the podcast companion did not carry (Morgan Stanley · Meta Marshall / Joseph Moore / Ella Tulchinsky / Antonio Jaramillo, "Bringing More Light to Scale-Up Networks: An Updated Scale-Up Primer", 2026-07-13).** The 07-17 bullet above came from the Tech Talk relay; the note itself dates the transition **per accelerator vendor**, which is the actionable part:
  - **[[NVDA]] leads.** **Rubin Ultra (2027) is an intermediate step** — GPUs *within* each 72-GPU rack stay on **copper NVLink** while **optical links connect multiple racks into a 576-GPU NVLink domain**. **Feynman (2028) is "the first meaningful deployment of fully optical scale-up connectivity" as NVLink domains expand to 1,152 GPUs** — but *"checks point to LIMITED DEPLOYMENT of Rubin Ultra with CPO, making Feynman, more likely scaling in 2029, as the adoption catalyst."*
  - **The rest follow "one to two years behind NVIDIA."** **[[AMD]] targets optical scale-up with its MI550 / MI650 platforms around 2027-2028** — the first per-platform optical timing for AMD anywhere on this wiki. **[[AMZN]]: Trainium 5 (post-2028) is "the earliest likely opportunity for AWS."** **[[AVGO]]'s custom-ASIC customers "also likely to adopt optics around 2028+,"** with Broadcom itself *"the most conservative on CPO, arguing continued advances in SerDes will extend copper's useful life."*
  - **[[GOOG]] is the structural exception, and MS states it more strongly than the relay did:** *"Google is the notable exception, as its TPU architecture relies on an **Optical Circuit Switch (OCS) Torus topology** rather than a traditional switched scale-up network, **which may delay or reduce the need for CPO**."* Google's **Palomar OCS** is cited as the production-scale reference. ⚠️ **This is the single most important qualifier on the CPO TAM: the second-largest accelerator platform by units may not need the product.**
  - **Copper's counter-attack is specific:** Broadcom *"has demonstrated next-gen technologies that still extend copper to **6m lengths**, challenging NVDA focus on CPO for cross-rack connectivity with Vera Rubin Ultra"*; the live debate is **448G SerDes, PAM6 modulation and 200G retimers**. Against that, the structural case: **HW FLOPS have grown 6,000,000x in 30 years (3x/2yrs) versus interconnect bandwidth 450x in 23 years (1.6x/2yrs)** — MS's cleanest framing of why the bottleneck moved to I/O.
  - **Sizing/adoption anchors:** scale-up **~$17bn by 2029 (last year's primer) → ~$73bn by 2030**, a 4x re-mark; **CPO cuts optical interconnect power ~50-80%**; **AECs cut interconnect power ~50% vs pluggable optics** with reach compressing **~7m → ~5m at 200G/lane**. Ratings as printed in the note: **LITE Equal-weight PT $900** (bull $1,125 / bear $500), **COHR Equal-weight PT $330** (bull $450 / bear $160), **GLW Equal-weight PT $180** (bull $230 / bear $72), **KEYS upgraded Equal-weight → Overweight, PT $350 → $400** (bull $500 / bear $220). ⚠️ **KEYS and SMTC have no wiki page** despite being, respectively, the note's only rating action and its named copper-plus-linear-optics play — flagged as a coverage gap, not routed.
- **NEW (2026-07-16) — JPM C2Q26 preview: valuations retreated but optical fundamentals unchanged → constructive on COHR/LITE (20-22x CY28); GLW placed on Negative Catalyst Watch (JPM · Joseph Cardoso "Hardware & Networking C2Q26 Preview," 2026-07-16).** JPM turns more constructive on Optical & Interconnect into earnings as the recent drawdown "opened interesting setups" — **COHR and LITE now ~20-22x CY28E with demand fundamentals unchanged** and high-margin DCI/OCS/CPO opportunities ramping in 2H26 (also Fabrinet ~20x CY28 with an AWS HPC/TRx + DCI turn). Flip side: JPM **placed GLW on Negative Catalyst Watch** — at **30x+ CY28 (implying ~40x for the optical business)** it "leaves little room for error," with **TV-display datapoints looking tougher into 2H26** likely balancing a strong Optical result. Favors Optical / EMS / Hardware into the prints. (JPM · Joseph Cardoso, 2026-07-16; see [../COHR.md](../COHR.md), [../GLW.md](../GLW.md), [../CRDO.md](../CRDO.md))
- **NEW (2026-07-17) — AMD MI500 may bring optical scale-up (CPO) into the roadmap — a new merchant-GPU optical demand vector to watch at the Jul-23 event (Jefferies · Blayne Curtis, 2026-07-16).** Into AMD's Jul-23 'Advancing AI' event, Jefferies expects new MI500 detail (CDNA6/2nm/HBM4E, 2027): **MI500 moves to a native UALink scale-up domain with 256 GPUs/rack, which may require a shift to optical interconnect.** Jefferies is watching whether **AMD commits to a CPO-based scale-up solution and who supplies the optical engine** — AMD is working with **[../ALAB.md](../ALAB.md) on UAL and [../AVGO.md](../AVGO.md) on ESUN** for the scale-up switch, and is an **investor/partner in Ayar Labs** for the optical engine. **Confirmation of optical scale-up at MI500 would be a meaningful read-through for the optical supply chain** (a second merchant-GPU vendor after NVDA moving toward CPO/optical scale-up, on top of the ASIC/switch programs this page tracks). (Jefferies · Blayne Curtis "Advancing AI 2026 Event Preview," 2026-07-16; see [../AMD.md](../AMD.md)) [Source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/UBS_on_AMD.html)
- **NEW (2026-07-16) — Citi debuts a formal InP (EML+CW) supply model: supply stays below demand through 2028; 6-inch the biggest structural lever (Citi · Papa Sylla "InP Supply Constraint Will Likely Persist," 2026-07-16).** Citi's new InP laser-supply model — paired with its China optical-demand model — has **total industry laser-chip supply nearly tripling from ~280M chips (2025) to ~1.1B (2028E, ~+60% CAGR)** yet still running **below demand for three years**. The **6-inch transition is the largest lever (a 6-inch wafer yields ~4.36x more chips than 3-inch)**; AXTI + Sumitomo (~50% of AI-related InP wafers) ramp ~3.5x by 2028; [COHR](../COHR.md) is the 6-inch leader / most-internal supplier. Gap detail: **EML supply ~33% below demand (2026) → 20% (2027) → 11% (2028)**; **CW less constrained in 2026 but widening to 19% (2027) / 55% (2028)** on CPO/NPO high-power demand + lower high-power CW yields; **100G/lane EML falls from ~55% of EML wafer allocation (2025) to ~5% (2028)** (200G EML die ~22% larger → offsets some wafer-output gains). China context: Feb-2025 InP export controls drove **6-inch InP wafer prices ~+250% to ~$5,000/wafer**, pushing [LITE](../LITE.md)'s backlog to 2028; TrendForce sees combined monthly EML/CW-DFB capacity reaching **50.7M units in 2026**. Chinese 200G-EML entrants (Everbright, Source Photonics, Accelink, Yuanjie, Sanan) are 1-2 yrs behind on qualification and none matches LITE's yield/volume at 200G. On the light source: **NPO in most cases needs a CPO-class laser (~400mW faceplate / ~150mW in-box)** where LITE is differentiated — even an NVDA NPO shift takes the same LITE ELS + laser chip, and NPO activity has *increased* since NVDA's CPO announcement. Reaffirms [COHR](../COHR.md) Buy/$420 and [LITE](../LITE.md) Buy/$1,100 (LITE FY27E EPS +6% on a +15% price increase; upside Catalyst Watch into the Jun-Q print). Corroborates the page's "own-the-light-source / supply-gated up-cycle" thesis with a formal supply-demand model. [Source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Citi_on_Inp.html)
- **NEW (2026-07-14) — Semtech an NPO/1.6T beneficiary; NPO framed as a *primary* scale-up architecture alongside CPO (GF Securities · Alicia Xia / Jeff Pu, SMTC initiation, 2026-07-14).** GF initiates Semtech (SMTC, no wiki page) at **Buy, TP $180 (25x FY28E P/E)** as a beneficiary of 1.6T + NPO. The theme read: **NPO adoption ramping from 2H27 removes the DSP, which raises the importance of analog front-end components (TIA/driver)** and lifts content — and GF argues NPO offers **lower packaging complexity, higher manufacturability and greater deployment flexibility than CPO, positioning it to emerge *alongside* CPO as one of the primary scale-up optical-interconnect architectures** (both NVIDIA and leading CSPs said to be evaluating NPO). Also: an industry-wide **200G TIA shortage** opens a 1.6T entry for merchant analog suppliers; CopperEdge ACC riding Google-TPU 1.6T build-out; the HieFo acquisition adds lasers. Reinforces the page's "NPO the interim step / coexistence" read (cf. Weckel, TSEM, UBS) from the analog-front-end angle. [Source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/20260714_GF_Securities_Co_Ltd_SMTC_Semtech_-SMTC_Buy--_Key_Beneficiary_of_16T-NPO_Ramping.html)
- **NEW (2026-07-14) — Aehr Test Systems: SiPho + optical-device burn-in demand inflecting — a supply-side leading indicator of the SiPho/1.6T transceiver ramp (Aehr Q4 FY26 earnings call + William Blair "The Inflection Arrives," Jed Dorsheimer, 2026-07-14).** Aehr (no wiki page) posted **record Q4 bookings ($60.7M, book-to-bill >3), record backlog ($100.6M)** and guided **FY27 revenue $130-150M (~$140M mid, 2.6-3x FY26)** — with **AI-processor wafer-level burn-in ~70%** of the mix and **silicon-photonics ~15-20%**; **optical-device test/burn-in for transceivers + chip-to-chip IO ~20% of FY26 revenue**, its lead SiPho customer ramping production and a newer networking customer taking delivery with more orders expected this CY. William Blair reiterates **Outperform** (guide 50% above its prior estimate; new AI/SiPho/memory customers excluded from guidance = upside). Read-through: rising demand for SiPho/optical burn-in + reliability screening is an *upstream* confirmation of the 1.6T/SiPho transceiver and CPO ramp this page tracks — reliability qualification (the gating item for CPO-into-scale-up) now pulling test capacity. Off-coverage theme read. [Source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Aehr_Test_Systems_The_Inflection_Arrives_New_AI_SiPho_and_Memory_Customers_Offer_Upside_to_Guide.html)
- **(2026-04-05, older) — Optical test/reliability is bifurcating into two economically distinct control points; scale-across inter-DC validation is the emerging next spend leg (Atlas Peak Research "Two Control Points in the Reliability Stack," 2026-04-05).** Frames optical-networking test-&-measurement not as one TAM but as **(1) upstream semiconductor/photonic-device screening** (remove weak wafers/die/modules before expensive packaging — Aehr's wafer-level burn-in, the higher-torque-but-narrower bet, gated on WLBI standardizing in silicon photonics/optical-I/O) and **(2) downstream link/system/fabric/deployment/operational validation** (signal integrity, interoperability, congestion, live reliability — **VIAVI**, the broader/more-monetizable exposure: F2Q26 rev $369M, NSE $292M, organic NSE +24% y/y, data-center ecosystem ~36% of rev). The net-new theme concept: **rising reliability spend spans both control points as architectures broaden across 800G/1.6T/scale-across/optical-I/O/XPO/selective CPO**, and **inter-data-center (scale-across / MOFN) optical validation is called out as the next spend leg** (VIAVI seeing emerging hyperscaler/SP field-instrument demand). Reinforces the reliability-qualification-as-CPO-gating-item thread (the same reliability inflection Meta data flagged) from the test-vendor side. Off-coverage theme read (VIAVI/Aehr — no wiki pages). [Source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Optical_Networking_Test_and_Measurement__Two_Control_Points_in_the_Reliability_Stack_Atlas_Peak_Research.html)
- **NEW (2026-07-14) — 650 Group / Alan Weckel expert call: the AI-networking market approaches ~$300B; CPO gradual (partial 2028, meaningful 2029), NPO the interim step, copper pushed as long as possible; OCS a stable ~$4.5–5B; 2030 DCI ~$30B in thirds (Alan Weckel · 650 Group expert call, notes by F. Watkins, 2026-07-14).** Weckel's networking/AI-infra webinar frames the whole optical-transition timeline: **CPO adoption is gradual, not binary — partial in 2028, becoming meaningful in 2029, crossover into the next decade** (this year still sub-800G; **3.2T connections announced next year but not material until 2029**). **NPO is the interim step before/alongside CPO — attractive especially for Amazon's early deployments; a customer that goes NPO may skip the first CPO generation.** Crucially, **optical does not replace Ethernet** — OCS, CPO and interposers "all end up being Ethernet-based," the end-state being interposers with everything optical off-chip. **Copper/AECs will be pushed as long as possible** (400G/lane copper "certies" proven in fall 2025, 800G certies likely next). Market sizing: **AI-networking TAM (all technologies) approaching ~$300B**; **Ethernet switching to exceed $200B well before the mid-2030s** (from ~$45B this year); **OCS a stable ~$4.5–5B market** (Google-Search leaf-layer, "not cannibalistic to Ethernet," but well suited only to private workloads/redundancy — not multi-tenancy, on packet-auditability grounds; early Anthropic-via-Google TPU builds likely OCS, migrating to Ethernet fabrics as fleets diversify); **2030 DCI market ~$30B split roughly in thirds** (transponders/line systems · switching/routing · ZR/ZR+ optics). On the ZR module map, **Marvell (via Inphi) + Cisco-Acacia were historically ~90% of the market; Ciena and Nokia (capacity via Infinera) now gaining share**. Read-through — see [../LITE.md](../LITE.md), [../COHR.md](../COHR.md), [../MRVL.md](../MRVL.md), [../CSCO.md](../CSCO.md), [../ANET.md](../ANET.md); corroborates the page's "CPO several years away / coexistence," own-the-light-source and copper-durability reads with fresh market-level sizing. [Source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/5455e244-9c8c-4c65-9884-6e6d1ffa28b4_Alan_Weckel_-_Group_VC.html)
- **NEW (2026-07-14) — Rothschild/Redburn strategy screen tags Lumentum + Coherent (+ Amphenol) among AI-capex "top Buys" (Rothschild/Redburn · Karamanos "AI Monetisation & Sector Rotation", 2026-07-14).** In a top-down strategy note (thesis: sector rotation reflects worry about AI-capex monetisation; AI revenues $789bn 2025 → $2trn 2028 justifying ~100GW), Redburn's North-American top-Buy list includes **Lumentum** ("core AI networking supplier — optical transceivers plus emerging CPO/OCS — adding ~80% capacity into surging demand, NVDA-backed") and **Coherent** ("the other optical-interconnect leader levered to AI DC networking, with upside from CPO, OCS and scale-across, and a $2bn Nvidia investment"), alongside connector maker **Amphenol** (33% organic on IT/Datacom AI connectivity). A strategy-level confirmation of the own-the-light-source + interconnect reads on this page — not fresh single-name PTs. See [../LITE.md](../LITE.md), [../COHR.md](../COHR.md), [../APH.md](../APH.md). [Source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Redburn_Research_3.html)
- **NEW (2026-07-13) — CPO optical-coupling *equipment* the emerging 2nd leg — All Ring's CPO coupling tools surge to ~29%/42% of sales by '27/'28 (from 2% in '26) as the industry preps capacity for commercialization (BofA · Cathy Hsu Liu, All Ring 6187 TT initiation, 2026-07-13).** A supply-side/capex read on the CPO ramp: BofA (initiating All Ring at Buy, PO NT$1,500) sees the back-end **optical-coupling equipment** step-up as a genuine 2nd leg of the advanced-packaging TAM — All Ring's CPO optical-coupling tool sales going from ~2% ('26) to ~29%/42% ('27/'28) as foundries/OSATs build CPO capacity for commercialization. Corroborates the "CPO real ramp 2028/29" timing on this page from the equipment-order side. All Ring off-coverage (theme read-through). (BofA · Cathy Hsu Liu, 2026-07-13; packaging leg on [cowos-packaging.md](cowos-packaging.md))
- **NEW (2026-07-14) — Jefferies optical expert call (host: Jefferies; expert "Rick Li," optics expert — named in the firm's own recap, confirming the earlier machine-transcribed version): "supply-constrained, not demand-constrained."** _(Expert view, NOT a house call; treat figures as directional.)_ Module TAM roughly **doubles ~$18bn (2025) → ~$35bn (2026)**, but every speed grade ships short: **800G ~10% short** (45mn+ demand vs 40–42mn shipped) and **1.6T ~30% short** (26mn vs 18mn). **2027 is already locked** with the module makers — **80mn 800G / 75mn+ 1.6T demand vs a ~55mn 1.6T ceiling → the ~30% shortfall persists a second year** (client guidance, not an estimate); Jefferies' own derived math: **2027 OT TAM could grow ~3x**. Two binding chokepoints, both **sold out through 2028 and un-substitutable by China: 200G EML and 1.6T DSP** (DSP >90% [AVGO](../AVGO.md)/[MRVL](../MRVL.md); EML from [LITE](../LITE.md)/[AVGO](../AVGO.md)/Sumitomo). **3.2T timing refined:** samples **4Q26**, small commercial shipments **4Q27** (MP 2028, ≤2.5mn units) — still EML-limited; the firm's recap adds a sharper technology call than the page previously had — **EML re-takes the lead at 3.2T** because SiPh's frequency ceiling isn't high enough there (SiPh wins 800G→1.6T, EML wins again at 3.2T), and even **TFLN** (the 3.2T modulation candidate) still needs an **external InP CW laser** for light generation — so **InP stays indispensable across every modulation path** (EML, SiPh, TFLN). **China-localization update:** the CW-laser leader is named **"YJ Tech"** — confirmed to be **Yuanjie**, already tracked on this page under that name (transliteration match, same displacement-of-Sumitomo thesis); net-new is a first concrete Chinese **200G EML** entrant — **DSBJ, targeting mass production in 2H26** — the first crack in the "no mature Chinese 200G EML vendor" moat (watch-item for [LITE](../LITE.md)/AVGO/Sumitomo EML incumbency). **GlassBridge spec detail:** confirmed **1D transmission only, max 24 fibers** — consistent with the page's existing read that it can't support NVDA/TSMC's 3D vertical-coupling CPO OIO. **AXTI named explicitly** among the InP-chain related names (with AVGO/Marvell/Sumitomo/Innolight/DSBJ) — direct read-through to [AXTI](../AXTI.md).
- **NEW (2026-07-14) — Tech-mix inverts to single-mode/SiPh; LPO is dead (Jefferies expert call, directional).** Single-mode → ~85%; **VCSEL/multimode collapses from a ~45% peak to <15%** (100G/lane + ~13m-reach capped, no 1.6T path; GaAs the materials loser 40%→15%). Within 1.6T, **silicon photonics >60% share** (from ~35% at 800G; Innolight guided ~80% SiPh on 1.6T) vs **EML 35–40%** — SiPh wins on power (~15%) + BOM (2–4 CW lasers vs 8); EML 10–15% pricier but better reach. **LPO has failed commercially** (<10% of 800G, zero at 1.6T; NVIDIA + Meta abandoned it after testing) — a clean **positive for DSP demand (AVGO/MRVL)**, pushing the industry to NPO/CPO. **XPO** (Arista, 64×200G pluggable; MSFT/GOOG/ByteDance testing) is the pluggable 3.2T answer (~late-2027/28, still DSP). **TFLN** the 400G/lane 2028+ candidate — where China's supply chain is most vertically integrated, a genuine 2028+ threat to the EML incumbents.
- **NEW (2026-07-14) — CPO tracking ~½ NVIDIA's targets; OCS a smaller fast compounder (Jefferies expert call, directional).** Expert CPO-switch ramp **18–20k (2026) / 120–150k (2027) / 300–400k (2028)** vs **NVIDIA's own 300k (2027) / 500–700k (2028)** — i.e. anyone modeling CPO on NVDA's roadmap is **~2× too high in 2027**; yield + packaging capacity the bottleneck (consistent with the "CPO real ramp 28/29" thread below). Read-through: negative for pure CPO-torque names, marginally positive for **pluggables holding share longer**. **OCS (Google-led):** ~18k (2026) → 40–50k (2027), doubling annually toward **~300k / ~$20bn** long-run TAM; MEMS the shipment leader (~60–70%); the gating item is operational (rewriting traffic scheduling around a slow-switching fabric), not hardware; **NVDA productionizing OCS in 2028 is the trigger.**
- **NEW (2026-07-14) — Supply-chain single-names + the China-localization scorecard (Jefferies expert call, directional).** Cleanest call is **[TSEM](../TSEM.md) (~70% of global SiPh-*foundry* capacity)** — tripling revenue **$220mn → $600mn in 2026** (sold out to 2027; **$290mn prepayments** vs a **$1.3bn book**) — **but share erodes to ~60% by 2027** as **TSMC/Samsung/GF/UMC enter** (SiPh is ~80% standard CMOS, so the yield moat erodes → not a monopoly multiple). **Soitec (SOI wafers)** the un-modeled chokepoint (<200k → ~2mn units in 4yr, ~10×; ~70% smart-cut IP). **Indium phosphide** value migrates EML → **CW light source** (every SiPh module needs an external InP source) → **CW/InP >$10bn by 2030** ([LITE](../LITE.md)/[COHR](../COHR.md)). **DCI = a second, under-owned demand curve:** GOOG has tendered ~$6bn, MSFT/AWS $4–5bn coming; DCI module market $3.5–4bn at +40% CAGR; **tunable lasers ~30% of DCI-module BOM and acutely short** (LITE/COHR vertically integrated capture it). **China localizes only outside the moat:** CW lasers **done** (Yuanjie displacing Sumitomo, into Innolight/Eoptolink/COHR), passives >85% Chinese, AWG (Shijia ~70%) — but **DSP and 200G EML are not localizable**. [GLW](../GLW.md) **GlassBridge** is less disruptive to FAU than the bear case fears (edge-coupling only, not the vertical coupling NVDA/TSMC use; not in NVDA's current CPO solution; not MP before 2028).
- **NEW (2026-07-13) — MS updated scale-up primer: the scale-up market is now a ~$70bn opportunity by 2030 (>4x last year's est); copper lasts longer, CPO is eventual (small 2028, meaningful only from Feynman/2029+); recent optical sell-offs "overdone"; OCP-October the next catalyst (Morgan Stanley "Bringing More Light to Scale-Up Networks", Meta Marshall/Joseph Moore, 2026-07-13).** MS's refreshed scale-up primer 4x's the TAM: the **scale-up (accelerator-to-accelerator) market is set to be a ~$70bn+ opportunity by 2030 ($73bn), over 4x the ~$17bn-by-2029 it estimated a year ago**, as capex + cluster sizes grow (Blackwell 72-GPU rack → Vera Rubin 144 → Rubin Ultra 576 → potentially >1,000 with Feynman). The central call: **copper remains the favored transmission medium for longer (latency/power/cost/openness), but optics in scale-up will happen** — meaningful CPO adoption is pushed out, with only **small adoption in 2028 and material adoption "no earlier than 2029" (the Feynman generation of NVDA)**. Roadmap: **Rubin Ultra (2027) = intermediate/hybrid** (copper NVLink within each 72-GPU rack, optical links joining racks into a 576-GPU domain), **Feynman (2028) = first meaningful fully-optical scale-up** (NVLink domains to 1,152 GPUs) — but checks point to limited Rubin Ultra CPO, so **Feynman scaling in 2029 is the real CPO adoption catalyst**. The four named catalysts for optical: **(1) multi-rack scale-up architectures, (2) rising electrical-I/O power, (3) rising bandwidth-density requirements, (4) more communication-intensive workloads.** Fabrics in flux: **NVLink / UALink / SUE (Broadcom Scale-Up Ethernet) / NVLink Fusion / PCIe (bridge)**; MS expects no uniformity. MS calls **recent optical sell-offs "overdone"** and flags **OCP (October) as the next catalyst** for CPO enthusiasm. Beneficiaries: **NVDA (Top Pick), AVGO (positive ahead of 2027-28 ASIC ramps + networking pull-through, most conservative on CPO), ALAB (OW — Scorpio X shipping / UALink), MRVL (share gains via Celestial-AI/NVLink-Fusion but sidelined on valuation)**; **GLW/LITE/COHR the clearest beneficiaries of the eventual move of optical into scale-up (evidenced by NVDA equity investments) — MS rebuilt its LITE/COHR/GLW models to incorporate CPO-penetration percentages**; Semtech offers copper (CopperEdge) + linear-optics exposure. Read-through — see [../LITE.md](../LITE.md), [../COHR.md](../COHR.md), [../GLW.md](../GLW.md), [../AVGO.md](../AVGO.md), [../MRVL.md](../MRVL.md), [../NVDA.md](../NVDA.md), [../CIEN.md](../CIEN.md); scale-up ASIC/fabric detail on [custom-asic-tpu.md](custom-asic-tpu.md). [Source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/TELECOM_20260713_0418.html)
  - **Read-through (no wiki page) — Keysight (KEYS) upgraded EW→OW, PT $350→$400 (Morgan Stanley, 2026-07-13).** In the same note MS **upgrades Keysight Technologies (KEYS) to Overweight (from Equal-weight), PT $350→$400**, on the thesis that a **test-&-measurement leader benefits from the *diversity* of scale-up architectures** — NVLink/UALink/Ethernet/PCIe, copper/NPO/CPO all need testing, and this note argues that diversity is exactly what plays out. MS sees room for further upward estimate revisions (AI/semis/A&D/edge-AI/6G incrementals), in line with historical strong-investment-cycle timing. _(KEYS has no wiki page — recorded as a plain-text read-through, per convention.)_
- **NEW (2026-07-11) — Mizuho names LITE and CRDO "critical suppliers" of the AI-DC-capex ramp: LITE PT $1,100 (sole-source CPO for NVDA Spectrum/Quantum-X + OCS at 3 hyperscalers, >50% EML share); CRDO PT $290 (AEC + optical DSP, TAM to ~$10B) (Mizuho "DC Capex Ramping to >$1.4T in 2028E", Vijay Rakesh, 2026-07-11).** Within its raised AI-DC-capex outlook (>$1.4T 2028E), Mizuho names **CRDO and LITE as "critical suppliers"** among the technology-enablers of the accelerating build (alongside GPU/ASIC leaders NVDA/AVGO). **LITE (Outperform, PT $1,100)**: market leader with **>50% share in high-speed EML**, optical-cable TAM growing **>70% CAGR (C24-29E)** and InP-laser deployments **>50% unit CAGR**, **sole-sourced CPO supplier for NVDA's upcoming Spectrum/Quantum-X switches**, with **OCS ramping at three key hyperscale customers (likely AWS/GOOGL/MSFT)**. **CRDO (Outperform, PT $290)**: SerDes-IP/AEC/optical-DSP innovator engaged with all seven major hyperscalers, TAM potentially **~15x its current >$1B/yr AEC run-rate**, LT TAM to **~$10B (>3x mid-2024's ~$3B)** across AEC/Optical-DSP/ALC/ZeroFlap/Omniconnect. Corroborates the page's own-the-light-source + copper-durability (CRDO/AEC) reads. (Mizuho · Vijay Rakesh "DC Capex Ramping to >$1.4T in 2028E; FCF Outlook Improving", 2026-07-11; see [../LITE.md](../LITE.md), [../CRDO.md](../CRDO.md)) [Source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/DC_Capex_Ramping_to__14T_in_2028E_FCF_Outlook_Improving.html)
- **NEW (2026-07-12) — the "copper lasts longer + NPO is the next dollar" consensus hardens; CRDO the pure-play beneficiary; Lumentum UHP laser reaffirmed over Coherent's (R&Co Redburn · Mike Harrison; Gaetano; Irrational Analysis, 2026-07-12/11).** Multiple independent reads converge on the same four-layer map: (1) **copper has a longer runway than the market thinks** — hyperscalers keep it relevant (cheaper/lower-latency), and NVDA is designing GPUs with **two physical ports** to stay flexible on copper vs optical amid launch-cycle delays; (2) **scale-up CPO is further out** than modeled; (3) **NPO is the next best thing and captures lots of dollars**; (4) **scale-across / DCI is a large emerging market** — CY2027 the ramp year. R&Co Redburn puts **CRDO (Buy)** at the center (copper hedge + differentiated **ZF Optics** with a failure-prediction software overlay; recent Oracle win; ~$600mn FY27 optical → >$1.3bn exit-rate springboard) (see [CRDO.md](../CRDO.md)). Gaetano frames the same map around SMTC (off-coverage). On the light-source debate, Irrational Analysis **re-affirms Lumentum's UHP laser as superior and calls Coherent's UHP laser "not viable"** — a negative read on [COHR](../COHR.md)'s CPO-laser positioning. (R&Co Redburn / Mike Harrison; Gaetano "Best Positioned Photonics Company?"; Irrational Analysis "NPO/CPO Update", 2026-07-12/11)
- **NEW (2026-07-11) — Susquehanna: TSMC COUPE FAU-constrained through YE27, high-volume ramp a 2028 story; NPO + 400G/lane pluggables adopted first, broad CPO at 3.2T ~2028 (Susquehanna · Mehdi Hosseini, 2026-07-11).** COUPE (TSMC co-packaged optics, PIC-EIC 3D/hybrid bonding) stays **FAU-constrained through YE27**: PIC capacity **<0.5K wpm now → only ~5-10K wpm by YE27**, with FAU (fiber-array-unit) challenges capping **COUPE shipments <0.5M units/qtr** and contributing to Spectrum-X yield constraints — i.e. on-package optics volume is supply-gated, reinforcing the page's "coexistence / CPO several years away" read. Industry adopts **NPO + 400G/lane pluggables first**, with broader CPO at the **3.2T node ~2028**. Competitive: **GLW's FAU/glass-packaging stack (co-developed w/ GlobalFoundries) framed as an alternative to TSMC CoWoS-L/COUPE** (extends the 2026-06-29 Corning Glass Bridge read in Key debates below), while a **faster COUPE ramp is neutral-to-negative for TSEM** as a standalone PIC supplier. (see [cowos-packaging.md](cowos-packaging.md), [GLW.md](../GLW.md))
- **NEW (2026-07-10) — Rumor: TSMC 2027 CoWoS capacity ≥200,000 wpm ("conservative"); AI-chip packaging in severe shortage, plants on 24-hr shifts (@dnystedt, 2026-07-10).** Media report (unnamed supply-chain sources) puts TSMC's 2027 CoWoS capacity at **≥200,000 wpm as a conservative estimate**, with AI-chip packaging still in severe shortage and nearly all TSMC plant-construction running 24-hour shifts; order allocation reportedly undecided (some fear TSMC holding back to force CoWoS-equipment price cuts). Read-through for optics: **CoWoS/SoIC is the shared advanced-packaging substrate COUPE optical engines ride on** (COUPE's PIC-EIC hybrid bonding sits in SoIC), so the same TSMC packaging capacity crunch gates optical-engine supply. (@dnystedt, 2026-07-10; see [cowos-packaging.md](cowos-packaging.md))
- **NEW (2026-07-09) — NVDA IR: CPO scale-out "in production" with Spectrum-X, NVLink CPO-or-copper an option from Feynman (2028); Credo optics inflecting to >$600mn FY27 (Citi · Atif Malik, 2026-07-08; UBS · Randy Abrams, 2026-07-09).** Per Citi's NVDA IR synthesis (2026-07-08): **CPO for scale-out is already in production with Spectrum-X** (adoption said to be high), and **from CY2028 with Feynman customers get the option of NVLink with CPO *or* copper** — i.e. on-package optics stays optional/coexists in scale-up, consistent with the page's "coexistence" reads. On the supplier side, **Credo (UBS Asia NDR, Not Rated, 2026-07-09)**: optical DSP/photonics guided to **grow 2-3x to >US$600mn in FY27** (optical CSPs, SiPh PICs, zero-flap transceivers each >$100mn; scaling to 100k/mo by year-end), while **AECs/copper still cover ~90% of DC connections** (100G/7m, 200G/6m, 400G/3-5m), and a new **Omni Connect** solution links up to **2TB LPDDR to a GPU** (~$2.5-3k content/GPU, >$1bn potential). TSMC PIC capacity ramping ~10k→25k wpm by 2028 (@dnystedt/@trendforce, 2026-07-08). (see [CRDO.md](../CRDO.md), [NVDA.md](../NVDA.md), [COHR.md](../COHR.md), [LITE.md](../LITE.md))
- **NEW (2026-07-06) — NVDA CPO scale-up delayed; GLW GlassBridge + glass substrates framed as CPO-era enablers; Optical driving GLW F2Q; Optical -14% on the week in the AI pullback (SemiAnalysis; BofA/Wamsi Mohan; JPM Hardware weekly, 2026-07-06).** Three same-day reads: (1) **NVDA's CPO scale-up roadmap slips** — **NVL576 (CPO between NVSwitches) delayed / small-volume, and the CPO NVSwitch is not landing until Feynman** (SemiAnalysis, 2026-07-06) — a further push-out of on-package optics in NVDA's scale-up fabric, consistent with the page's "CPO several years away / coexistence" reads. (2) **[GLW](../GLW.md) GlassBridge** (high-density fiber alignment for co-packaged optics) plus **glass substrates in 2029-30** are framed as CPO-era enablers, with **Optical supporting GLW's F2Q rev/EPS at/above guide** — BofA (Wamsi Mohan, reiterate BUY, PO raised to $243 from $223, price $196.79, 37x C28E EPS $6.50) has Enterprise ~$1.1bn (+45% y/y) and Carrier ~$932mn (+17% y/y), solar the near-term offset (BofA · Wamsi Mohan, 2026-07-06). (3) **JPM Hardware weekly (2026-07-06):** the Optical sub-sector was **-14% on the week** in the broad AI pullback, with **positive sentiment on [LITE](../LITE.md)** — JPM contrasts the tape weakness with still-strong underlying demand signals (JPM Hardware weekly, 2026-07-06).
- **NEW (2026-07-06) — SemiAnalysis sizes *pure* scale-across content at ~$3B (2026) → ~$14B (2028), split into ZR/ZR+ pluggables + Optical Line Systems (@SemiAnalysis_ thread, 6/7).** SemiAnalysis: scale-across (data-center-to-data-center) "should meaningfully drive content on networking equipment, and more specifically on **ZR/ZR+ coherent pluggables** and on **Optical Line Systems (OLS)**"; it estimates **pure scale-across content ~$3B in 2026 growing to ~$14B in 2028**. This is the most aggressive 2028 mark on the page and the first to split the content specifically into ZR/ZR+ pluggables + OLS — steeper than the other scale-across sizings here (650 Group's ~$500M→~$10B and Rothschild's ~$7B/2030, both different base/target years). Read-through to [CIEN](../CIEN.md) (coherent-pluggable + OLS leader, exiting 2026 ~#1 in 800G coherent pluggables) and to [COHR](../COHR.md)/[LITE](../LITE.md) (coherent optics + the InP/laser supply behind ZR/ZR+). _(Tweet-thread fragment, 6/7; source date approximate — flag if the note carries a precise date.)_
- **NEW (2026-07-03) — Arista's "XPO" could beat CPO to mass production in 2027, pushing out CPO timing (DIGITIMES report, 2026-07-03).** DIGITIMES notes **Arista Networks unveiled XPO at OFC 2026** — unlike CPO (disruptive), **XPO is an engineering-led improvement of *pluggable* optical modules** that preserves the existing module ecosystem (high bandwidth density, space savings, easier maintenance, low migration barrier) while not eliminating the physical limits of electrical signal transmission. CPO remains the acknowledged long-term end-state, but **XPO already has public support from Microsoft and multiple suppliers expect mass production in 2027** — so **XPO could win data-center share first and potentially push out CPO commercialization timing**. At OFC 2026, camps diverged: **NVIDIA still invests in CPO R&D**; **Tencent and Alibaba Cloud prioritize Near-Packaged Optics (NPO)**; Arista extends pluggables via XPO — i.e., a multi-technology gap-filler period before CPO matures. _(Reinforces the page's "coexistence, not winner-take-all" and "CPO several years away" reads — and is a near-term positive for pluggable/transceiver suppliers [COHR](../COHR.md)/[LITE](../LITE.md) vs the CPO-displacement bear.)_
- **NEW (2026-07-02) — UBS lays out the CPO adoption timeline: scale-out from H2'26, scale-up NVLINK from Rubin Ultra, GPU-CPO by 2029 (UBS "Asia Semiconductors — Sector Keys", Section 4, 2026-07-02).** UBS (Sunny Lin) frames CPO as "lighting the path to AI acceleration," with a **three-year tech-maturity ramp** and staged adoption: **(1) scale-out** — Nvidia may push the CPO switch (Spectrum-X) **from H2'26** for Rubin racks; **(2) scale-up** — a hybrid approach first upgrades the **NVLINK switches to CPO from the Kyber architecture (Rubin Ultra, Feynman)** while GPU compute trays stay copper; **(3) scale-up CPO for GPUs** matures for adoption **~2029E (if not earlier)**. UBS sizes the CPO opportunity as part of an optical market growing to **~US$32bn by 2030E, of which CPO could reach ~20% (~US$7bn)**; assuming ~50% of Rubin rack shipments late-2026→2027 use CPO for scale-out implies **~3m optical engines (OEs) in 2027E** and **0.4-0.5kwpm of SoIC capacity**, with OE demand inflecting to **30-50m+ units in 2028E** as Nvidia migrates the scale-up switch. **TSMC is the key enabler** (EIC/PIC/OE stacking + supply-chain integration → CPO strengthens TSMC's Cloud-AI moat); **ASE/SPIL support FAU/OE attach on substrates**, USI supports ASE's CPO packaging + laser modules, final test at ASE/KYEC; testers Teradyne (PIC/OE/FT), Advantest (EIC wafers), Chroma (OE/laser source); Hon Precision FT handlers; external laser source via Lumentum + Coherent (Nvidia's US$4bn / US$2bn-each investment). CPO is positive for **ABF-substrate makers** (optics on substrate) and TSMC, mixed for traditional transceiver suppliers (UBS "Asia Semiconductors — Sector Keys", 2026-07-02). _(Corroborates the "coexistence, not winner-take-all" and NVDA-forcing-the-pace reads already below; the ~3m-OE-2027/30-50m-2028 units and 2029 GPU-CPO timing are concrete new UBS marks.)_
- **NEW (2026-07-02) — ECTC 2026: Marvell + Lightmatter bring optical interconnects onto the package (SemiAnalysis "EMIB-T Roadmap / ECTC 2026", 2026-07-02).** SemiAnalysis's ECTC roundup flags optical interconnects / CPO as "another prevalent theme," with the packaging-side proof points now landing: **Marvell** showed its **Optical Multi-Chip Interconnect Bridge (OMIB)** and **Photonic Fabric** (both acquired via Celestial AI) — a PIC embedded in an organic RDL interposer only where needed (electrical bridge elsewhere), claiming **1.8 Tbps/mm² bandwidth density** and removing shoreline limits by routing both on-package die-to-die and external optical links through the same bridge. Marvell's tested optical engine uses **electro-absorption modulators (EAMs) rather than micro-ring modulators** (better thermal stability, wider wavelength range — but "difficult to manufacture at scale"), and its thermal data shows the PIC rising **<5°C on substrate vs ~25°C on interposer** under full XPU load, arguing EAM bias voltage tracks XPU power-state changes faster than ring modulators' heater-feedback loops. **Lightmatter** detailed the **Passage M1000** multi-reticle photonic interposer assembly — a **four-tile ~2100mm² interposer** with 15 ASIC chiplets, >95% electrical assembly yield despite ~59µm reflow warpage (magnetic-fixture fix), validating cooling of **680 W from a concentrated test-chip area** in a package designed for >900 W. In the near term "**vertically stacked optical engines like TSMC's COUPE are more achievable than OMIB-style connections or a full photonic interposer**" (SemiAnalysis "EMIB-T Roadmap / ECTC 2026", 2026-07-02). _(Reinforces the MRVL/Celestial-AI scale-up-CPO thesis and the TSMC-COUPE/SoIC + hybrid-bonding routing already tracked below; see [cowos-packaging.md](cowos-packaging.md) for the packaging detail.)_
- **Demand is not the debate; supply is.** Across every call the refrain is identical: orders strengthen weekly, the question is capacity. Chinese transceiver leaders said by end-Q1 they were "confident to fulfill the entire 2027 demand" and are struggling only to expand capacity; the upstream chokepoint is the **EML laser / CW laser shortage**, plus DSPs and even passive components (Andy/MS optical panel, 2026-05-07). Most Chinese transceiver names *missed* Q1 on delivery despite linear demand — a supply miss, not a demand miss, with procurement inventory building on balance sheets (Andy/MS, 2026-05-07).
- **800G → 1.6T transition is the volume event, and it's bigger than 400G.** Ciena expects to exit 2026 at or near #1 share in 800G coherent pluggables (was late to 400G, ~30% share); the 800G market is larger than 400G because port counts hold while price-per-plug rises, and 1.6T high-performance modems are already shipping (Ciena is sole vendor with a 1.6T modem vs the market's 1.2T) (Ciena/Brodi, 2026-06-12). Cisco/Acacia is pushing a 1.6T PAM4 receiver — its first organic inside-the-DC receiver — that 650 Group calls a multi-billion-dollar, "millions of units" opportunity on vendor-diversity demand (650 Group, 2026-06-04).
- **InP is the structural chokepoint and it does not clear.** Lumentum guides InP-laser demand growing ~85% CAGR to end of decade while world InP capacity only 2x–4x over three years — so the imbalance persists; LITE + COHR are "completely sold out this year, next year and most of 2028," with supply response gated by multi-year fab conversions (Mike/Rothschild, 2026-05-19). Lumentum confirms take-or-pay LTAs locked into 2028 and "increasingly confident" into 2029; it is auctioning incremental units above committed take-or-pay at healthier pricing (Lumentum/Michael, 2026-05-07).
- **NVDA is forcing the CPO pace; AVGO/Cisco are measured.** NVDA pushes CPO hardest because it needs the manufacturing *volume* in place ahead of the inevitable scale-up transition; Broadcom and Cisco have native switch silicon in-house and don't need to be early, so expect a higher CPO mix at NVDA next year than at AVGO/Cisco even for equivalent products (650 Group, 2026-06-04). Cisco's first CPO is expected in Silicon One on a ~102T/51.2T switch for scale-out (650 Group, 2026-06-04).
- **NVDA–Corning partnership reframed the CPO supply chain.** Corning entered a commercial + technology + equity deal with NVDA (NVDA warrant up to ~$3.2B of stock; a direct multi-billion-dollar prepayment) to 10x US connectivity capacity and +50% fiber, explicitly aimed at "what's next going inside of the box" CPO — underpinning Corning's plan to build a **$10B photonics business by 2030** (Corning IR, 2026-05-12). NVDA struck a parallel laser purchase-agreement with Lumentum; LITE reads Jensen "buying fibers" as a tell that NVDA is preparing to own scale-up cluster wiring (Lumentum/Wupen, 2026-05-07).
- **Scale-across has inflected from a standing start.** 650 Group sizes scale-across going from ~$500M last year to a ~$10B market; Ciena is seeing a second major hyperscaler place scale-across coherent-pluggable orders, where RLS cuts fiber-pair count ~64x and 800G halves the modem count vs 400G (~50k vs ~100k modems for a 22-petabit link) (650 Group, 2026-06-04; Ciena, 2026-06-12).
- **CPO reliability has crossed the threshold that tips it into scale-up — the 2028/29 inflection now has a catalyst.** Redburn (Mike Harrison's "Tripping the Light Fantastic", 1 May 2026) had framed pluggables' serviceability edge as offsetting CPO's power efficiency; the update is that **recent CPO link-failure-rate improvements (Meta data) tip the balance toward wider scale-up adoption by 2028/29**. The enabling step is in packaging: the optical engine's **Photonic IC (PIC) and Electronic IC (EIC) must be hybrid-bonded via direct Cu-to-Cu** to kill parasitics at the boundary and hit **200 Gbit/s per lane** — TSMC's COUPE platform does this in SoIC (Redburn "BESI", 2026-06-15). This is the supply-chain mechanism behind the optics-into-the-rack thesis, and it routes incremental TAM to TSMC SoIC + hybrid-bonding equipment (BESI).
- **Pump lasers are a *second*, distinct InP constraint — and "scale-across" turned it into a growth leg.** Lumentum holds **70–80% of the pump-laser market** (and similar on ITLAs), is "the kingmaker in terms of allocation," and is **behind demand by ~50–60%** — even more acute than EMLs — driving **mid-double-digit % price increases** on pumps and a guided **3–4x output/revenue over 4–6 quarters**; scale-across orders now come from **Nokia, Cisco, Molex and Ciena**, not just Meta/Ciena (Hurlston, JPM LITE mgmt call, 2026-05-07; LITE IR call, 2026-05-11). Pumps share LITE's Santa Fe fab with phase-one CPO, forcing Caswell UK faster into CPO service (JPM LITE mgmt call, 2026-05-07).
- **Optical's supply discipline looks *less* tight than HDD on capacity — by design.** Unlike the HDD duopoly (explicitly not adding unit capacity), optical vendors are **signing LTAs up to five years and adding InP unit capacity** (brown- and green-field) — the multi-pronged capacity ramp (vs HDD's one- or two-product ramp) is the swing factor, and customers are now trying to **lock in supply** rather than chase demand visibility (BofA optical supply-chain call, 2026-06-12).
- **6-inch InP yield is the COHR-vs-LITE fork.** Coherent reports **every product on 6-inch (EMLs, CW, photodiodes) yielding better than prior gen**, with 6-inch >50% of InP volume from the Sep-Q and a path to **>42% GM**; Lumentum stays hesitant on 6-inch (its 4-inch yields are already good, so relative uplift is smaller). COHR is guiding **a quarter ahead on 2x InP cal-26 + another 2x cal-27** (JPM "Networking recap", 2026-05-08; BofA CIEN supply-chain call, 2026-06-12).
- **400G-per-lane copper is settled — and it's a CPO release valve.** The standards bodies have resolved that **400G-per-lane can still run copper** (Broadcom pushing full-copper scale-up at 400G/lane), so **CPO concern is "both overblown and much further into the future"**; co-packaged-copper benefits the connector vendors (Molex, Amphenol, TE, TTM) and lets CPO ramp "more naturally" rather than being forced. New laser entrants (AAOI, MACOM, Semtech, Lightmatter, Nokia) pile in as the CPO laser TAM scales **low-hundreds-of-millions → ~$1B**, raising a real **glut risk** as everyone adds capacity (650 Group, 2026-05-26).
- **CPO/SiPh is a named SoIC growth driver into 2027–28 — capacity is being built for it.** Fubon: silicon-photonics chips become "a big SoIC application in 2027," and TSMC SoIC capacity scales **45k → 65k → 90k wpm by end-FY26/27/28**; CoPoS (20–30k wpm by end-2028, a TSMC priority project at VisEra, MP targeted 2H28) is being readied partly for NVDA's Feynman platform features (Coupe 2.0/LPU). On the optical/networking-interconnect side, **Broadcom's transceiver demand "will grow tremendously in 2027" driven by hyperscalers such as Meta**, handled by **ASE (FoCoS revised up 30k → 45k wpm by end-2027)**; V10 TPU adopts **400G SerDes** (Fubon "Semi 2027 outlook", 2026-06-05). Redburn corroborates: TSMC accelerating AP7 SoIC capacity, CPO a "continuation in growth for SoIC."

- **NEW (2026-06-30) — Tower Semiconductor (SiPh + SiGe foundry) maps the pluggable → scale-up → scale-across → CPO/NPO progression (Susquehanna · TSEM call, 2026-06-30).** In a Susquehanna-hosted call, Tower (TSEM, off-coverage) — which supplies **silicon-photonics (the transmit modulator) + silicon-germanium (the driver/TIA)** into the optical-transceiver pluggable market — framed its opportunity set moving from **scale-out pluggables (its focus today) → scale-up (replacing copper inside the rack) → scale-across (linking clusters) → eventually CPO / NPO**. On NPO specifically: it puts the same components as a pluggable module **onto the board**, shifting where the electrical-to-optical transition happens (fiber carries the signal with far lower loss than a copper trace). Corroborates the copper→optical scale-up/scale-across ladder this page tracks, from the SiPh/SiGe-foundry supply side (complements the SIG/Hosseini TSEM read of 2026-07-01 already in prose below). (Susquehanna · TSEM, 2026-06-30; see [../GLW.md](../GLW.md)) [source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_06_30_tsem_susq_30_jun_26.html)
- **NEW (2026-06-23) — Google Principal Architect (expert call): scale-up optical (LPO/CPO) is a "completely new cost item"; NVIDIA integrating CPO into NVLink switches (16 NVL72 racks → one scale-up domain); EML lasers "sold out for the next year" (Google · Arthur Khessin, expert call, 2026-06-23).** A hyperscaler-side confirmation of the scale-up-optics thesis: Google's Arthur Khessin described **scale-up optical content (LPO + CPO) as a "completely new kind of cost item,"** putting optics on the **GPU-to-GPU (NVLink) fabric to replace copper** for tightly-coupled GPUs beyond one rack — **~10x the bandwidth of scale-out networks**. NVIDIA is driving the reference designs and **plans to integrate CPO into NVLink switches in future generations (e.g. linking 16 NVL72 racks optically into one scale-up domain)**, with most hyperscalers/neoclouds following NVIDIA's lead (Google, with its own networking stack, the exception). Component tightness: **EML lasers are "tight" and "sold out for this next year."** Beneficiaries flagged: Lumentum, Coherent. (Google · Arthur Khessin expert call, 2026-06-23; see [../LITE.md](../LITE.md), [../COHR.md](../COHR.md), [../NVDA.md](../NVDA.md); DC-capex leg on [ai-datacenter-power.md](ai-datacenter-power.md)) [source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_06_23_Call_Google_-_Principal_Architect_Office_of_the_CTO_Arthur_Khessin_Memory_Cycle_2306_email.html)
- **Scale-up switching + scale-up CPO are the near-term "explode" trade — merchant goes zero-to-big in two years (BTG/LightCounting expert "Bob", 2026-06-25).** LightCounting's read: **scale-up switching, then scale-up CPO right behind it, are "very much going to explode in the near term"** — starting with [ALAB](../ALAB.md) **Scorpio X ramping now**, followed by scale-up CPO ramping **particularly with the [MRVL](../MRVL.md)/Celestial AI acquisition toward the end of next year** — i.e. these go from zero to very large merchant numbers over the next couple of years. Most early CPO designs use **external laser sources supplied by [COHR](../COHR.md) and [LITE](../LITE.md)** ([AVGO](../AVGO.md) "supposedly" has high-power lasers but isn't seen in near-term designs); for CPO one external laser feeds multiple ports, so it needs **very-high-power lasers where the supplier count is essentially two (possibly three)** and ASPs are higher — reinforcing the own-the-light-source thesis. The SiPh piece routes to MRVL (Celestial AI) plus privates Ayar Labs / Lightmatter; VCSEL-based scale-up (no external laser) is so far a China / near-package-optics interest, not adopted by NA hyperscalers (BTG "LightCounting Optics/CPO Expert Call", 2026-06-25).
- **CPO is reliability-driven now, but NVDA is still pushing ahead of end-demand (BTG/LightCounting, 2026-06-25).** Corroborates the Meta reliability-inflection thesis: [AVGO](../AVGO.md)'s earlier CPO designs got little traction ("seeding the market," small deployments), but **Meta lab-testing of AVGO's latest switches found them far more reliable than pluggable-optics systems**, making CPO interesting for *reliability* (critical to AI training), not just power. The caveat: **[NVDA](../NVDA.md) has not yet published reliability data for its own CPO**, so it remains "more of a push from NVIDIA" — NVDA is preparing the supply chain for volume (the rationale behind its COHR/LITE investments) **"but we don't think the demand matches up"** with the supply being lined up just yet; large-customer pull "we don't necessarily see just yet" (BTG "LightCounting Optics/CPO Expert Call", 2026-06-25).
- **1.6T DSP: design wins ≠ share — AVGO competitive but not at the volume sockets yet (BTG/LightCounting, 2026-06-25).** First-gen 1.6T DSPs ran too hot, so **all suppliers had to redo 3nm designs**; MRVL was first to market but didn't ship in meaningful volume, letting AVGO catch up — **AVGO and MRVL now essentially in lockstep on 1.6T time-to-market**. But near-term 1.6T demand is **primarily Google (uses MRVL) and NVDA (uses its own internal DSP, 5nm shipping now, 3nm coming this year)**, so despite a competitive product **AVGO has little near-term 1.6T share**; AVGO should gain share rapidly as 1.6T broadens (incl. Tomahawk-6 switches) beyond Google/NVDA. On MRVL: surprised that besides Celestial AI scale-up CPO, MRVL guided its **legacy in-house PAM4 ~200G/lane optical engine to ~$150M revenue next year (same number as Celestial)** — MRVL now offering a broad portfolio (Celestial, VCSEL arrays, micro-LEDs) and letting customers choose, which may be why NVDA treats MRVL as ecosystem rather than a Broadcom-like competitor (BTG "LightCounting Optics/CPO Expert Call", 2026-06-25).
- **AI networking TAM raised to ~$316bn (from ~$240bn) on optics/switch ramp (BofA "AI 2030: Stronger for Longer", 2026-05-13).** BofA's CY30 AI-networking TAM goes to **~$316bn from ~$240bn** (AI switching $129bn, SmartNIC $78bn, connectivity/other $110bn of which optical $88bn at +42% CAGR / copper $22bn at +61%), inside a ~$1.7Tn total AI-DC-systems TAM; CPO transceiver TAM is modeled small but rising ($0.2bn 2026 → ~$1.1bn 2028). The note lifts MRVL PO to $200 (from $125), COHR to $400 (from $365), maintains LITE $1,100 — flagging **COHR initial scale-out CPO UHP lasers shipping 1H27** as an NVDA-supply-chain tailwind (BofA "AI 2030: Stronger for Longer", 2026-05-13).
- **Tower Semiconductor fireside chat details the foundry-side heterogeneous-integration roadmap (SIG/Mehdi Hosseini, 2026-07-01).** SIG hosted TSEM management on transceiver-manufacturing dynamics: **pluggables remain the primary Si-Ph/Si-Ge demand driver at 800G** (no negative demand impact from the CPO narrative, consistent with the theme's existing "coexistence, not winner-take-all" read), with content per transceiver rising as the industry migrates 800G→1.6T; **LPO and pluggables could be among the first form factors to reach 400G/lane**. **NPO transceiver adoption is expected to begin by YE2026 and accelerate into 2027** alongside 1.6T pluggable traction — a specific timing data point for the scale-up transition this theme has been tracking qualitatively. Management still frames **true optical CPO commercialization as "several years away"** and expects pluggables to persist, with CPO capturing only part of total transceiver growth (consistent with the page's existing bear case that CPO-in-scale-out saves only ~2-3% of cluster power). On the laser side: TSEM's opportunity is **heterogeneous integration of III-V (InP) lasers onto its silicon-photonics platform** — sourcing InP wafers with epitaxial layers already applied from **IQE** (not bare substrate from Sumitomo) — targeting pluggables and NPO initially, independent of CPO adoption; the **III-V-on-CMOS integration challenge has been resolved**, with new customer relationships (Scintil on the laser side, Oarele, Salient) rather than existing ones, and **management named Nvidia as the party driving the customer roadmap**. On wafer size, management called **200mm "perfectly adequate and the better platform on cost"** for current Si-Ph/Si-Ge products (nitride-carried laser layers work fine at 200mm); 300mm is reserved for larger-form-factor/integrated-receive products and its added capacity **awaits a METI (Japan) grant approval**. On the path to 400G/lane, TSEM cited **two competing approaches: an all-silicon route via a [COHR](../COHR.md) partnership (Tower SiPho + Coherent's design)** and a **heterogeneous InP route via an OpenLight partnership**, plus investment in lithium niobate/polymers. Separately, TSEM flagged that **resolving fiber-array-unit (FAU) challenges — work associated with [GLW](../GLW.md) — would be positive for Tower and accelerate scale-up**, though Tower itself has not announced FAU participation (SIG "Tech Talk: Inside the Transceiver Manufacturing Supply Chain," Mehdi Hosseini, 2026-07-01).
- **Conor's Asia trip surfaces a SiPh equipment-spend play and new optical-component names (Conor, 2026-06-23).** Redburn's Asia trip introduced **silicon-photonics chips as a new SOIC use case** (confirming the optics-into-the-rack / TSMC COUPE thesis from the equipment side) and several off-coverage names as ways to play CPO/SiPh equipment spending: **Ferrotec** makes **thermoelectric (TEC) modules for optical** with ~**70% share** (Lumentum ~20%, also supplies Coherent/II-VI and InnoLight/Eoptolink) at ~**30% margins** — a coherent-optics-leveraged business growing faster than the group; **Kolon Industries (Korea)** makes the **MPO specialty chemical for CCL** (current customers iTech/EMC/TUC), revenue doubled to ~160B, under Doosan qualification (could 4x to 300–400B if qualified); and lab-equipment house **M.A.Tech** (top-10 customers, +51% growth) surfaced **Hamamatsu Photonics (Japan)** — an **IR/CCD detector** maker — as a probe/test supply-chain idea (alongside FormFactor/MPI on probing and Keysight/Anritsu on test) (Redburn "Conor O'Mara Asia Trip Takeaways", 2026-06-23). These are plain-text off-coverage names (no wiki page).

## Transceiver market — global optical-module TAM / shipments / ASP (GS model, 2026-08-10)

_Ingested 2026-08-11 from a pasted exhibit ("Global Optical module market opportunity"); broker + date confirmed by Felipe at ingest (**GS, 2026-08-10**; analyst not captured). ⚠️ The underlying note is NOT on disk — pull the PDF into `_inbox/` when available. Full three-panel model transcribed below; internal consistency verified at ingest: every speed subtotal foots to the printed total, and annual TAM = units × blended ASP in all four years._

- 🔴 **The shape: the dollar TAM peaks in 2027.** $34.2bn 2025 → $50.9bn 2026E (+49%) → $72.6bn 2027E (+43%) → **$69.1bn 2028E (−5%)**. Units still grow in 2028E (528M → 556M, +5%) — the decline is entirely price/mix (blended ASP $137 → $124, −10%). **This is the first bank model on this page that puts a date and a size on the C28 pricing rollover Jefferies warned about** ("classic capacity overshoot into C28… the pricing gains that drove margin expansion would be the first casualty" — Jefferies · Blayne Curtis, 2026-08-05). Note GS's mechanism: within-speed ASP deflation (2028E: 800G −25%, 1.6T −27%, 3.2T −25%) plus legacy decay — not an AI demand miss.
- 🔴 **The AI-speed dollar pool goes FLAT 2027E→2028E.** 800G+1.6T+3.2T: $13.2bn 2025 → $32.3bn 2026E → $55.5bn 2027E → $55.7bn 2028E (+0.3%). Everything 2028 adds in units (3.2T 13.1M → 28.2M), pricing gives it back — for the module makers ([[LITE]], [[COHR]], [[AAOI]], Innolight/Eoptolink) 2028 is a mix-and-share fight, not a pool-growth year. Consistent with MS/Marshall's "2028 scale a little lower, not later" (2026-07-28) and the Jefferies overshoot bear; sits AGAINST BofA's compounding optical curve (~$88bn optical by CY30 at +42% CAGR, "AI 2030", 2026-05-13). Both retained — the 2028+ slope is the live disagreement.
- **The speed torch-pass is fast.** 1.6T overtakes 800G in dollars already in 2026E ($18.7bn vs $13.5bn — 37% of TAM) and peaks 2027E at $27.4bn (38%); 800G dollars peak 2026E at $13.5bn. **3.2T appears in 2027E at $13.3bn — 18% of the TAM in its first modeled year (13.1M units) — reaching $21.6bn / 31% in 2028E.** ⚠️ Direct disagreement with 650 Group/Weckel (2026-07-14): "3.2T not material till 2029." Logged, not resolved.
- **SiPh crosses majority of dollars in 2027E**: 28% (2025) → 43% → 56% → **62% of value by 2028E**; EML mirror 72% → 38%. ⚠️ **Basis trap: the unit-based "SiPh penetration rate" row is a different number — 6% → 10% → 15% → 18%.** The ~3.4x wedge is SiPh concentrating in the high-ASP 800G+/1.6T buckets — quote value-share and unit-penetration as different quantities. Near term the model still has EML at 57% of 2026E value, consistent with the EML-shortage/LTA thesis carried on this page (LITE ~60% EML share, capacity LTA'd through FY27 — Aurelion/PhotonCap, 2026-07-29); the 2027-28 SiPh flip is the "SiPh commoditizes and coexists" path (Teradyne/Arcuri, 2026-05-04) showing up in a bank model.
- **ASP mechanics — enter at ~2x, then deflate hard.** 1.6T enters at $840 blended 2025 ≈ 1.9x 800G's $440 — ✓ corroborates the "1.6T ≈ 2x ASP uplift" already carried here (Barclays via Jefferies previews, 2026-08-05). 3.2T implied ~$1,020 in 2027E (computed: $13,340m ÷ 13,067k — the cell is not printed in the exhibit) → $768 printed 2028E (−25%). Deflation accelerates with generation age: 800G −10% / −17% / −25% over 2026-28E; 1.6T −13% / −18% / −27%; below-400G blended $47 → $31.
- ⚠️ **Model-structure trap for anyone lifting quarterly numbers: the quarterly "ASP" panel = current-quarter revenue ÷ PRIOR-quarter units** — verified across every speed and quarter (e.g. 800G 2Q25 "ASP" $1,635 = $2,120m ÷ 1Q25's 1,297k units; 1.6T 3Q25 $2,416 = $1,469m ÷ 2Q25's 608k). GS is recognizing revenue one quarter after shipment. Annual ASP IS same-year $÷units. Do not divide quarterly TAM by same-quarter units, and do not quote the quarterly "ASP" as spot pricing.
- **Cadence into the prints**: 2Q26E TAM $12.0bn (+51% y/y) with 1.6T ($3.7bn) passing 800G ($3.5bn) in-quarter for the first time; the year is heavily back-loaded — 4Q26E $15.9bn (+25% q/q), 1.6T $7.6bn (+72% q/q) on 11.0M units. That implied 2H acceleration is the demand curve underneath the "beat and raise" setups for [[LITE]] (08-11) and [[COHR]] (08-12).
- **Cross-checks vs marks already on this page**: (a) MS "Global AI Transceiver USD TAM to expand >4x from 2025 to 2028" (via 2026-05-15 briefing) — reconciles ONLY on the AI subset: GS's 800G+ pool $13.2bn → $55.7bn = 4.2x; the TOTAL market is just 2.0x because below-400G+400G decay ($21.0bn → $13.5bn). Basis lesson: ">4x" is the AI slice, never the whole market. (b) JPM "optical datacom market tracking to $50bn+" (JPM · Cardoso via Meyers, 2026-08-05) — same zip code as GS's 2026E $50.9bn TOTAL, but the GS total still carries $16.9bn of below-400G legacy (800G+ subset: $32.3bn) — scope unverified, flagged rather than equated.
